Stormlight Season 1's TV Rating Addressed By Brandon Sanderson

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Stormlight Archive might have a very deliberate rating.

Brandon Sanderson has addressed the possible TV rating for The Stormlight Archive season 1, unpacking his expectations for the series. The upcoming Apple TV adaptation of The Stormlight Archive pulls from the pages of Sanderson's epic fantasy series of the same name. The book series takes place on the world of Roshar, which is constantly pummeled by violent storms across various continents. Its story centers around the Knights Radiant, a magical order of beings whose combined powers are destined to help save the world from certain destruction.

Now, speaking on his Halloween livestream Brandon and Emily Sanderson Present: A Night of Fan Mail on Halloween 2026, Sanderson spoke about his expectations for what The Stormlight Archive TV show will be rated. Responding to the question, "What do you intend for the content rating your adaptations to be?" he said he wants his adaptations to be "Marvel movie level," implying a PG-13 or TV-14 aim.

But he says he wouldn't be surprised if The Stormlight Archive winds up with a TV-MA rating. This is because of major battle sequences in season 1 that could end up pushing the boundaries of the show beyond a possible TV-14 ratings. Check out what Sanderson had to say below:

Most of them would be Marvel movie level. Snapshot has constantly been under option, that's a serial killer thriller, that would probably be R-rated if it were made. Stormlight, I mean, there are some battle scenes in there that I don't know would be... it's probably going to be TV-MA. But I think there's going to be some war scenes in Stormlight. We'll have to see.

Based on his statement, it appears Sanderson is aiming for other Cosmere installments, such as his upcoming Mistborn movie, to be more aligned with a PG-13 typical of Marvel movies. However, because The Stormlight Archive can sometimes deal with more intense themes while also featuring battles that could grow violent onscreen, its TV-MA rating seems almost inevitable. Given the complex history of Roshar coupled with the many tribulations of its characters, it wouldn't be surprising if the series dug into its darker themes in more detail.

Although it's unclear how dark the fantasy epic novel series will get when translated from page to screen, Sanderson's biggest concern for content appears to only be violence. This means the adaptation likely isn't going to include TV-MA content that's too graphic outside of battles. But, since these can be fairly intense in the novel, and will likely be even flashier when adapted onscreen, it makes sense for Sanderson to consider a TV-MA rating for the books.

Alongside the development of The Stormlight Archive, Sanderson is also working on the second draft of his Mistborn movie script. His young adult novel series Skyward has also been picked up for adaptation by Tomorrow Studios, best known for their work on the recent live-action One Piece for Netflix. The Stormlight Archive is just one of many onscreen projects Sanderson is juggling, but it's most certainly one of the biggest in his forthcoming catalog.

With the series poised to have a TV-MA rating once the adaptation comes out, it remains to be seen just how hard The Stormlight Archive will push the envelope. But, if it's anything like the books, then violence will be the only major factor that keeps it from achieving a TV-14 rating instead. It may be just one element, but it will keep the Apple TV adaptation faithful to the source material, not compromising Sanderson's vision for the story.

Corpse Bride's Live-Action Remake Chances Get Major New Tim Burton Update After 21 Years

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Tim Burton has addressed whether Corpse Bride could finally be resurrected over 21 years after the original movie.

While Tim Burton's name has been synonymous with Disney's The Nightmare Before Christmas film since its 1993 release, he was credited for the story and characters and served as an executive producer, not the director. It wasn't until 2005 that Burton directed his first full-feature stop-motion animated movie with Corpse Bride, a fantasy musical about a shy groom who accidentally marries a deceased woman while practicing his vows. The critically acclaimed film has remained an annual Halloween season staple, and fan-driven demand for a live-action adaptation has stayed just as strong.

In an interview with Vanity Fair about Tim Burton's new The Nightmare Before Christmas collection with fashion company Loewe, the filmmaker was asked about whether a live-action Corpse Bride movie will ever come to fruition. While Burton didn't give a clear affirmative, the magazine noted that he also didn't say "no," with Burton instead stating that he's "gone through too much to go, 'Okay,'" as other projects have "mutated" and changed throughout the years:

Tim Burton: "I’ve got thoughts swirling around and all sorts of things going on, but I’ve been through too much to go, 'Okay.' I keep it open, and I’m just finishing something, so I’m in an open spot now, and I’m really excited to look for what the next thing is."

Burton didn't give a concrete answer about whether a live-action Corpse Bride movie is one of the potential projects "swirling around," but his response is more hopeful than his stance on a possible The Nightmare Before Christmas sequel, prequel, or remake. In previous interviews, Burton has given a more solid "no" to questions about bringing back the 1993 animated film for another screen adaptation, noting how "important" and personal those characters and the story are to him.

Throughout over a four-decade career, legendary director Tim Burton has only remade one of his previous films. In 2012, he collaborated with Disney to release a feature-length stop-motion animated remake of his 1984 live-action short film Frankenweenie. Additionally, Burton has only ever directed two sequels to his projects, with the first being Batman Returns (1992) and the latest being Warner Bros.' hit fantasy Beetlejuice Beetlejuice (2024). As such, though a live-action Corpse Bride remake wouldn't be unprecedented, it would be a rare move for Burton.

Currently, the only Tim Burton project in post-production awaiting release is Wednesday season 3, with the super-hit Netflix series having wrapping filming on its latest episodes at the end of September. Netflix has yet to officially announce a release date for the Addams Family spinoff's third outing, but the new season is expected to premiere in 2027. Meanwhile, Burton has a few other films reportedly in the works, including a post-World War II movie with Kazuo Ishiguro and a remake of the 1958 sci-fi cult classic Attack of the 50-Foot Woman.

The original Corpse Bride movie's voice cast included several frequent Tim Burton collaborators, being led by Johnny Depp as Victor Van Dort and Helena Bonham Carter as the eponymous character, Emily. The vocal ensemble also featured Emily Watson, Richard E. Grant, Albert Finney, Joanna Lumley, Tracey Ullman, Paul Whitehouse, Christopher Lee, Michael Gough, Deep Roy, and Danny Elfman. However, a potential live-action Corpse Bride adaptation would be expected to feature a brand-new lineup of actors.

Newcastle Star Causes Chaos After Bow and Arrow Incident

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

A current player on the books of Newcastle United has caused chaos in England after being spotted with a bow and arrow. The Premier League star in question is defender Amar Dedic.

The Bosnian international signed for the Magpies in the summer from Benfica in a deal worth just under £30m. The right-back was consequently reunited with Matthias Jaissle, his former coach at both FC Liefering and RB Salzburg.

Dedic has been a key player for his new side as well, with six starts in the league to his name from the opening nine games, producing two assists in that time. Newcastle, however, will be concerned to learn the defender's car was recently at the centre of a major police operation this week.

Scholes specifically chose one Arsenal player ahead of Ballon d’Or winner Harry Kane.

As reported by the Daily Mail, Dedic sparked police alarm on Wednesday after being spotted carrying a bow and arrows in Northumberland. A concerned member of the public reported the Newcastle defender and a friend after they were seen with the weapon near a reservoir in the countryside outside Newcastle.

It's said that 'at least six patrol cars' were then involved in the search for the footballer, as they were dispatched to the area in Harlow Hill. Not all the police cars were marked, but it's understood that some were armed response units.

Dedic and his companion were finally intercepted around seven miles away in the exclusive Darras Hall estate. Sources described a dramatic swoop on Dedic's vehicle before officers searched the car and seized a bow and arrows, with the 24-year-old questioned about his activities.

Adding to the drama, Newcastle goalkeeper Mark Gillespie and former defender Paul Dummett arrived at the scene while police were speaking to Dedic. It is said that the pair were simply in the area by chance and stopped to offer assistance.

After speaking to the two men, officers were satisfied there was no threat to the public, with police subsequently stating that the equipment was believed to have been intended for recreational use. Dedic is said to have fired some arrows while in the countryside, although it remains unclear what he was aiming at. Hunting any animal or bird with a bow and arrow or crossbow is illegal in the UK.

A Northumbria Police spokesperson has now shared a statement on the matter. It explained that enquiries remain ongoing into the incident:

"Shortly after 1.55pm on Wednesday, we received a report of two men carrying a bow and arrows while walking along a track by the reservoir on Military Road in the Harlow Hill area.

"Officers attended and subsequently stopped a vehicle in the Darras Hall area and seized a bow and arrows. At this stage, it is understood they were for recreational use and there was no threat to the public. Enquiries are ongoing."

The Daily Mail report also claimed that Dedic will not face disciplinary action from Newcastle over the incident. That said, there is the internal feeling within the club that the player was naive to believe he could walk around the countryside carrying a bow and arrow.

The Bosnian is expected to be available for Saturday's Premier League trip to Fulham.

David Lynch-EP’d Thriller ‘Hyena’ Sets Q1 2027 Start As Kiernan Shipka & D’Pharaoh Woon-A-Tai Replace Previously Announced Leads

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Psychological thriller Hyena, one of the final films late director David Lynch signed onto as an executive producer, has announced a Q1, 2027 start date, and new co-stars.

Industry and Longlegs star Kiernan Shipka and Emmy nominee D’Pharaoh Woon-A-Tai are set to costar alongside further fresh cast additions, César Award winner and Golden Globe nominee Edgar Ramírez, and Independent Spirit Award nominee Rosemarie DeWitt.

Shipka and Woon-A-Tai replace previously announced leads Archie Madekwe and Havana Rose Liu. Related Stories Casting Finn Wittrock & Supriya Ganesh Horror Thriller 'Portrait' From Robert Schwartzman Heads To AFM Casting John Gore Studios Boards Peter Hoar's 'A Waiter in Paris' With Leo Woodall As Feature Begins Filming & Adds Raft Of French Talent

Scott Coffey directs from a screenplay he co-wrote with novelist Blair Mastbaum. Production is slated to begin in Spokane, Washington early next year. Watch on Deadline

Additionally, WME Independent has joined Upgrade on sales, with the partners introducing the project to buyers at the AFM.

Hyena follows Genevieve (Shipka), who brings her enigmatic new boyfriend Alexei (Woon-A-Tai) home to meet her seemingly idyllic family.

Their attempts to welcome him begin to fracture as Alexei turns his attention toward the family, working his way into them and exposing long-buried secrets, desires and hypocrisies, until Genevieve is the only one left willing to see him clearly, and saving what remains of her family demands an unspeakable sacrifice.

The film is among the last credits for Lynch, the iconic filmmaker who died in January 2025. Coffey appeared as Wilkins in Lynch’s Mulholland Drive, and following this collaboration, Lynch became a mentor to Coffey and was closely involved in the early development of Hyena.

Lynch, takes an executive producer credit on the project, alongside Oscar-nominated producer David Lancaster (Whiplash, Nightcrawler). Lancaster will produce alongside Jeff Elliott (Canary Black).

“Hyena is a very personal film. I’ve always believed the most unsettling horror isn’t supernatural — it’s the moment grief and love turn feral, when we stop pretending we aren’t wild. Kiernan Shipka, D’Pharaoh Woon-A-Tai, Edgar Ramírez, and Rosemarie DeWitt will bring extraordinary depth, intelligence, and vulnerability to the movie,” Coffey said, whose previous features include Ellie Parker and Adult World.

Shipka’s credits include Osgood Perkins’ Longlegs, Gia Coppola’s The Last Showgirl, Macon Blair’s Idiots, HBO’s smash hit Industry and AMC’s Mad Men.

Woon-A-Tai is known for his performance in FX’s Reservation Dogs. He was most recently seen in Andrew Haigh’s A Long Winter, Darren Aronofsky’s Caught Stealing and Alex Garland’s Warfare.

Ramírez’s recent credits include Jacques Audiard’s Oscar-winning Emilia Pérez, and Eli Roth’s Borderlands. He is set to headline season two of HBO’s acclaimed series Task.

Known for Rachel Getting Married and La La Land, DeWitt recently starred in Smile 2, can currently be seen in the Netflix crime drama Untamed opposite Erica Bana, and will be seen in the upcoming Cannes entry Diamond alongside Andy Garcia, Brendan Fraser, Bill Murray & Dustin Hoffman.

Upgrade and WME Independent are handling sales on Hyena and will introduce the project to buyers at the AFM.

Teyonah Parris To Co-Star With Denzel Washington In Christopher McQuarrie’s Thriller For United Artists

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

EXCLUSIVE: Teyonah Parris is set to star opposite Denzel Washington in Amazon MGM Studios’ and United Artists’ untitled thriller with Christopher McQuarrie on board to direct. The film, which is penned by Scott Barkan and Gregg Zehentner, will be released in theaters on October 1, 2027.

The plot centers on a former police detective serving a life sentence who is diagnosed with terminal brain cancer. Granted compassionate release with six months to live, he returns home to the estranged daughter he left behind and a grandson he never knew existed. He only wants to make amends and forget his dark past, but his dark past has not forgotten him.

McQuarrie will also produce alongside Heather McQuarrie along with Stuber and Nick Nesbitt, who produce through United Artists; Joseph Restaino for Hungry Bull Productions; Reza Moheet and Norman Aladjem for Mainstay Entertainment; Katia Washington; and Tommy Harper. Hungry Bull’s Tony Stopperan, Mainstay’s Tim Honigman and Joe Guest will serve as executive producers. Watch on Deadline

After breakout roles in Marvel’s WandaVision and They Cloned Tyrone, Parris is risen the ranks of leading ladies in this town and clearly caught the eyes of producers and execs for this film after impressive audtioon for the role.

She most recently starred as Charmaine in Apple Original Films’ action-adventure Matchbox, alongside John Cena and Jessica Biel. She has also joined the cast of Gavin O’Connor’s upcoming drama Running and is set to star in Don’t Go Gentle, a poignant contemporary drama examining themes of identity, belonging, home, and legacy.

In the Wake of the 2026 Midterms, Can We Reframe the Patriotic Song?

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

When Brandy Clark arrived on set to film the lyric video for her new single “American Roots,” her makeup artist had an idea. He wanted to paint her face and hands red, white, and blue, to riff on the song’s ideas of envisioning a loving, inclusive America where everyone, including immigrants, belong.

Clark was a little nervous at first, but it didn’t stop her from moving forward. “I’m so associated with the rainbow flag,” Clark, whose excellent new album The Bird is out in January, tells Rolling Stone. “But we deserve this. My fans deserve to see this.  And a lot of people in my family, my dad being one of them, served in the military for that flag. That’s all of our flag. MAGA does not own the flag.”

It’s a sentiment that a lot of Americans have been wrestling with as the Trump administration has worked to turn the flag into a symbol not of pride, but exclusion — and for country fans, one that was co-opted in a post 9/11 landscape where jingoistic songs passing as patriotism became radio gold. In the wake of the November midterms, however, when a blue wave saw Democratic gains in states once dismissed as lost causes, can artists change their relationship to patriotic songs?

Long before Tuesday’s elections, it’s something that Clark was thinking about with “American Roots,” along with others like Mickey Guyton, the War and Treaty, and many more country and roots artists interested in reframing exactly who gets to own the idea of patriotism. In a clip from Newport Folk Festival making the viral rounds this week, Joan Baez, alongside Mavis Staples, Nathaniel Rateliff, Brandi Carlile, and others, close the festival with their take on “America the Beautiful,” introduced with some potent words. “This song is something that we are taking back,” Baez said.

Part of this process, according to the War and Treaty’s Michael Trotter Jr., is divorcing patriotism from partisan (and often hateful, exclusionary) politics. Zac Brown tried to claim his performance of the National Anthem at President Trump’s UFC match on the White House lawn last summer was just “patriotism, not politics.” That was hard to square, however, when the country singer stood on the same stage where heavyweight fighter Josh Hokit later celebrated his win by shouting, “Michelle Obama is a man.” Editor’s picks The 250 Greatest Albums of the 21st Century So Far The 100 Best TV Episodes of All Time The 500 Greatest Albums of All Time 100 Best Movies of the 21st Century

“I take great pride in being able to say that I served this country and served it well,” Trotter, a combat veteran of the Iraq war, told Rolling Stone. “So, I am ashamed that people would hide behind the term ‘patriotism.’” Sheryl Crow, in a visit to Nashville Now, agrees.

“There are words we use against each other to create sides: ‘fake,’ and ‘woke,’ and ‘patriot,’” Crow said. “The word ‘patriotic’ and ‘patriot’ really is offensive to me. Because I feel very patriotic about my country, but I’m not a conservative. I’m a middle-of-the-road pragmatist. But [patriotic] is used for one category of people and it’s used against others and I take offense with that.”

That approach has been a part of the country music playbook for decades now, and was on Clark’s mind when she started writing “American Roots,” a song that is gentle in its messaging: we’re all Americans, with equal rights to walk on our hallowed land, wherever you may come from originally. It hinges on a lyric in the bridge. “When you set foot onto that shore,” she sings, “it’s no more mine than it is yours.”

“To me that song is a conversation between two people, and two people who might not agree,” Clark says. “Like, tell me about your family tree, when we start digging where’s it going to lead? It’s about finding commonality throughout the whole song. And then when you get to the bridge, it’s like, okay, here it is. My hope is that someone who doesn’t agree with that statement has found enough of themselves in the song that they can at least hear it.” Related Content 'This Had Better Work': Why This Folk-Country Band Sold Their House to Go on Tour How Abdul El-Sayed Overcame the Hate and Won Big in Michigan Jimmy Kimmel Celebrates Midterm Election ‘Blue Tsunami’: ‘Now the Red Wedding Begins’ Keisha Lance Bottoms Makes History as First Black Woman Elected Governor

The 2026 midterm elections have, for many, made that dream feel all the more realistic. Voters vehemently rebuked, with some important exceptions, Trump’s hateful policymaking, with Democrats taking both the house and the senate. Scores of historic elections will put the first Native American female governor, Black female governor, Muslim senator, openly gay male senator and more in power, crafting a government that actually reflects the people living within in. Whether it leads to more artists feeling emboldened to reclaim their version of a patriotic anthem is still yet to be seen, but with songs like “American Roots,” the tide seems to be turning. Everyone should get to partake in this musical tradition, regardless of political beliefs. Trending Stories Appeals Court Says Lawsuit Over ‘The Pitt’ Can Proceed, Cites ‘Numerous’ Similarities to ‘ER’ Nicki Minaj Is Going Down With Trump’s Ship After Nearly Every Candidate She Backed Lost As Nicki Minaj Rallied for Trump's GOP, Her Lawyers Told Court She Still Owes Them $295,000 Bob Dylan Meets With Pope Leo XIV in a Private Audience

Guyton, back in 2021, was already pushing the concept on “All American” from her debut LP Remember Her Name, an album all about claiming and reclaiming space in both country music and the country itself. “We got the same stars, the same stripes, just wanna live that good life,” she sang. “Ain’t we all? Ain’t we all all-American?” A version of that sung by a white conservative man would have been a radio hit, but Guyton’s song has more impact than a fleeting Number One: it changes the definition of who gets to own a patriotic song.

Clark is working on that, too. “I’m a proud American,” she says. “Why can’t I be patriotic by saying we all belong?”

Liam Neeson's 2022 Flop Gets A Shot At Redemption With Fox's Dark Thriller

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Although one Liam Neeson action thriller from 2022 never came together quite right, Memory has gotten a shot at redemption with a dark new show from Fox that converts its story into a long-form format. Sometimes, even great adaptations can withstand a second go-around. When 1994’s Anne Rice adaptation Interview with the Vampire assembled an astounding cast including Tom Cruise, Brad Pitt, Antonio Banderas, and Stephen Rea, it seemed like the beguiling movie might never be outdone.

However, after a few decades of killer vampire shows like From Dusk Til Dawn: The Series and Buffy the Vampire Slayer, AMC took another shot at Interview with the Vampire with a longer series based on the same book that debuted in 2022. The result was a genuine masterpiece that made major stars of its leading men, Sam Reid and Jacob Anderson, as well as reintroducing Rice’s classic Gothic tragedy to a new generation of viewers.

Since the original movie was already perfectly good and its TV remake was even better, it stands to reason that a TV show remaking an already-flawed movie is even more understandable. Released in 2022, the Liam Neeson vehicle Memory was an action thriller from Just Play Dead director Martin Campbell. Based on the Jef Geeraerts novel De Zaak Alzheimer, the movie had a killer pitch, but squandered it with forgettable action sequences and a lack of depth. Fortunately, Fox’s Memory Of A Killer can now do justice to the novel.

Created by Ed Whitmore, Fox’s Memory Of A Killer stars Patrick Dempsey as the hitman Angelo Doyle. While Angelo’s brother has already lost his memory to Alzheimer’s disease, Angelo is only just starting to see the effects of the disease materialize in his own faltering memory. Angelo has managed to meticulously hide his secret criminal career from his family for years, but when a hit is put out on his pregnant daughter, his fading memory starts to blur his worlds together.

While Memory Of A Killer has a bigger cast of characters than 2022’s Memory, that is not the only difference between the two movies. In Memory, Neeson’s aging hitman lives in Mexico and is sent on one last job in El Paso, whereas in Memory Of A Killer, Dempsey’s Angelo is based in New York City. Angelo maintains an elaborate second life posing as a photocopier salesman, while Neeson’s lonely protagonist is a full-time contract killer.

Both Dempsey and Neeson's projects are based on 2003’s Belgian thriller The Alzheimer Case, directed by The Loft’s Erik Van Looy. However, while Memory is almost too straightforwardly similar to The Alzheimer Case, Memory Of A Killer changes a lot of plot details. These range from changing the setting to introducing the double life story, to adding Angelo’s backstory as a widower.

The end of Memory Of A Killer season 1 threw Angelo a curveball when one villain’s death only opened up another can of proverbial worms with the revelation of a mysterious document. Meanwhile, Angelo learns that his dementia is getting worse near the end of season 1, meaning it seems increasingly unlikely that Memory Of A Killer’s protagonist will be able to keep his work and personal lives separate as the show continues.

While Memory Of A Killer season 2’s release date has not yet been announced, the Fox show is expected to return early in 2027 as part of the network’s mid-season schedule. Deadline reports that season 2’s cast will introduce Soul Food’s Boris Kodjoe as Tre, the Fourth Musketeer of Angelo’s friend group, while Meta Golding will play Angelo’s childhood love Mel when Memory Of A Killer season 2 arrives next year.

‘Primetime’ Star Skyler Gisondo On Pushing Beyond His Comfort Zone & Teasing What’s Next For Jimmy Olsen

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

You’d think in the last year, especially given that he’s coming off a successful outing as Jimmy Olsen in James Gunn’s Superman, that Skyler Gisondo would imagine himself as pretty cool. Though, jokingly, by his own account, nothing quite topped appearing in Phoebe Bridgers’ “Lost Boys” music video. “I never felt cooler in my life than being in a Phoebe Bridgers music video,” he tells Deadline, recalling how the appearance prompted texts from people he hadn’t heard from in 15 years, saying, “Dude, I had no idea you were so cool.”

On the day of our conversation, Gisondo is in Florida, perched at a nearby playground beneath a vibrant blue sky. Bright-eyed, handsomely freckle-faced and dressed in colorful attire, he is all smiles and eager to chat. Ironically, it’s a decidedly sunny setting for a conversation about Primetime, Lance Oppenheim’s dark, satirical thriller. Related Stories News Robert Pattinson To Receive 40th Annual American Cinematheque Award News 'Toy Story 5' Writer-Director Andrew Stanton Wants To Bring Nuance To The Conversation Between Kids & Technology

There is a familiar eagerness in Dan, the aspiring actor Gisondo portrays, but the similarities largely end there. When Dan takes a job as a decoy for Chris Hansen (Robert Pattinson) on To Catch a Predator, his excitement about the opportunity gives way to an increasingly disturbing reality. Dubbed “Decoy Dan,” he finds himself in the middle of a glossy, exploitative operation that is a far cry from the acting career he imagined for himself. For Gisondo, the role presented its own challenge. He admits that reading the script initially left him questioning whether he could pull it off. “It was the most challenging thing I’ve ever done, and ultimately the most gratifying by far,” he says. Watch on Deadline

A day after our conversation, Gisondo received a Gotham Award nomination for Outstanding Supporting Performance, becoming Primetime’s sole acting nominee. Below, he discusses stepping outside his comfort zone, returning to comedy with Focker-In-Law, and what lies ahead for Jimmy Olsen.

DEADLINE: Help me unravel the timeline. You were in Phoebe Bridgers’ “Lost Boys” music video, directed by Lance Oppenheim. But you had already been in Primetime by that point?

SKYLER GISONDO: Right. The music video was maybe six months after or perhaps even longer. But Primetime happened because Lance had seen some of my work and wanted to talk. I was elated because I had watched Some Kind of Heaven and thought, “This guy is clearly an unbelievably gifted filmmaker.” For Lance to have already achieved such a high level of mastery at such a young age is incredible. It usually takes directors a long time to get there. I had never worked with a director my age. So, we immediately developed a real friendship, and I was in disbelief that he was interested in me for the part. I remember asking him in our first meeting, “Dude, what have you seen of mine that convinced you this would be a good idea? I don’t want to talk myself out of this incredible opportunity, but at the same time…”

Phoebe, though, to my understanding, had never really acted before, but she knew Lance organically and went quite far back. She was amazing, especially given that she had never acted before. In addition to being a generationally talented artist, she’s just the best and such a great hang. We all became friends. When her music video came together, I thought I had never felt cooler in my life than being in a Phoebe Bridgers music video. I had people texting me whom I hadn’t heard from in 15 years, saying, “Dude, I had no idea you were so cool.” It gave me an incredible amount of cred, and it was so much fun.

Thankfully, I hadn’t been overly exposed to her music before the movie because I would’ve been freaking out the entire time. I would’ve been fangirling and wouldn’t have been able to keep it together. Before the music video, I went through her whole discography and, like everyone else, was in awe of her. It was such a fun experience. Lance and our other friend, Pablo [Rochat], directed the video together. I’m still bumping that album [Lost Weekend] nonstop. It’s so good.

I vacillated wildly between thoughts of major imposter syndrome. I’d never done anything like this. Am I capable of it? Do I have this thing in me that he thinks I do? I’m not convinced that I do. But ultimately, those thoughts are a distraction, and you try to shut them out.

DEADLINE: You’ve played a lot of characters who are funny, charming, and usually serve as conduits for the audience. Dan has some of that earnestness before he goes into darker territory. Did you see Primetime as an opportunity to challenge how people perceive you as an actor, particularly after a year that also includes Jimmy Olsen in Superman?

GISONDO: I was really excited by the opportunity to do something I had never done before. I was initially unsure I could do it after reading the script. I’d heard actors I love talk about being drawn to challenging projects, and I almost never understood that. I thought, “Why would you want to show up scared to work every day?”

For the first time, I understood it. A lot of that was Lance instilling an incredible amount of confidence in me from the beginning. He’s the kind of filmmaker you trust. A lot is out of your control as an actor, so it doesn’t really behoove you to overly concern yourself with where everything is going to land or what the perception might be. All you can do is put one foot in front of the other, be present, and look at everything through the lens of your character as you’re reading the script. That’s really all there was. I vacillated wildly between thoughts of major imposter syndrome. I’d never done anything like this. Am I capable of it? Do I have this thing in me that he thinks I do? I’m not convinced that I do. But ultimately, those thoughts are a distraction, and you try to shut them out.

In this case, with Dan specifically, those thoughts were almost helpful in a meta way because there was so much from my own life to draw from. Having started acting when I was so young, I understood that quiet desperation and eagerness Dan has when he first comes into the picture. When you start thinking about the big picture, it’s easy to get overwhelmed. It really is one scene at a time. It’s, “What are we doing today? What is this scene? What is this moment?” That’s why, as an actor, working with the right filmmaker is everything. What Lance and his editor, Dan Garber, ultimately did was take this very small piece that you contribute as an actor and make it part of a much larger puzzle. When it all works, it’s incredible. The whole becomes so much greater than the sum of its parts.

I was surprised, too. Lance has such a distinct visual style, as does his cinematographer, David Bolen. Watching the movie for the first time, I had never seen myself that way. It had never occurred to me that I could exist in that kind of movie or world. It was an immensely formative experience because I found that it was the most challenging thing I’ve ever done, and ultimately the most gratifying by far. When you see a movie work, it really is a magic trick. Everybody has an idea of what they’re doing, but you can’t possibly understand how you fit into the whole until you see it come together. Everyone brings their own thing to it, and then you have a filmmaker like Lance who creates something so exciting.

It really lit a fire under me moving forward. I don’t know that it would’ve been lit otherwise. I know I want to pursue that feeling of uncertainty and fear in the work.

DEADLINE: OK, we have to talk about this friendship dynamic between Troy and Dan. I don’t think Dan needed to apologize to Troy at the end. Troy was helpful in the beginning, but I think as the movie goes on, Troy becomes passive-aggressive or slightly envious. What’s your take on their relationship?

GISONDO: Hm. You know, I think they have a very odd, special relationship. Eric [Rahill] was so unbelievably funny and is just a singular kind of actor. He brought so much to that character; ultimately, a good amount made it into the movie, but a lot of it also didn’t because it was so funny. I don’t think anyone knew where to put it, almost because he could take a scene and go somewhere completely new every time. Should Dan have apologized though? You know what… I think it’s wrong of Dan to come at him. I do think Troy wants what’s best for Dan, and that’s why he’s not as immediately excited about the opportunity as Dan is. He’s trying to make sense of what it is, while Dan is still in a place where he’s ready to compartmentalize and get excited about whatever it is. He has this job; he’s going to be on TV.

His buddy is being a good buddy and saying, “It doesn’t really sound like acting, man. It doesn’t really sound like what I know you want to do.” So I think Dan is in the wrong. Honestly, I hadn’t thought about it, but I think Dan’s in the wrong there.

I had never seen myself that way. It had never occurred to me that I could exist in that kind of movie or world. It was an immensely formative experience because I found that it was the most challenging thing I’ve ever done, and ultimately the most gratifying by far.

DEADLINE: Working with Robert Pattinson, what kind of conversations did you two have when Dan has to step in and essentially become Chris Hansen while he is having a panic attack outside?

GISONDO: What’s funny is that when you watch the scene in the movie, it seems obvious what it’s about: Dan doing his best Chris Hansen impersonation. But going into the day, it was clear on the page that Dan was trying to fill the role of hosting the show and moving it forward. It wasn’t clear that he was literally going to try to impersonate Chris. As much as I had thought about that scene, it was only about half an hour before we were about to shoot that it occurred to me that it would be great if Dan literally tried to channel Chris. Rob was on set shooting something else, so I flagged him down and said, “Hey, dude, can we run through this? Can you read my lines? How would you do this scene?”

He was fired up about the idea, so we spent 15 minutes with him basically being my acting coach for his version of Chris Hansen. I’m very lucky it worked out that way and that he was on set. I think the idea came to me when I saw what Lance and Bolen had cooked up with that camera move, that iconic move where it comes around to Chris. It struck me: “Oh, this is that moment. Dan needs to…” I think Dan is excited by the opportunity to do some real acting, to channel a character, because he still doesn’t really understand Chris’s motivation or what he’s doing. Earlier in the movie, Chris says to him, “Nothing I do is acting, Dan.” But I think Dan is still trying to find his place in the show, and he sees this as an opportunity to create a character in that moment.

DEADLINE: Where do you see Dan by the end, when he gets his SAG card? Has he learned a lesson from everything that happened?

GISONDO: It’s interesting because that final scene wasn’t originally in the movie. Lance called me a few months after we wrapped and wanted to shoot another scene with Dan. We talked about what that could be, and we came up with the idea of him getting his SAG card. It was originally scripted so that when someone asks, “Are you an actor?” Dan thinks about it for a minute and then says, “Yes.” We did that a couple of times during this one-day pickup in LA, but something wasn’t working. Lance suggested, “Why don’t you try saying no?” We tried a few takes like that, but it didn’t feel right. Then he said, “Let’s try to just look at him. Just look at him for a while.”

I was already playing the scene as if Dan were somewhat traumatized by the experience. There’s this strange man knocking at his door, bringing up all these uncomfortable memories. We found that was the most interesting approach. Dan hadn’t considered whether or not he was an actor anymore in the year since the show or whatever. But now, finally, the thing he always wanted is in the palm of his hand. I don’t know that Dan knows how he would answer anymore. I think the ending is perfect. Lance’s instincts were totally right. I don’t think Dan knows anymore.

DEADLINE: So, next we’ll see you back to being charming again in Focker-In-Law. Talk about joining this franchise and its cast, which includes Ariana Grande, Robert De Niro, and, of course, Ben Stiller?

GISONDO: It was awesome. Those movies meant so much to me as a kid. I think we all grew up with them. Ben is somebody who’s been an enormous influence and inspiration to me, and a real hero of mine. He and I had worked together years earlier on Night at the Museum. I played his son, and I was 17 at the time. It was a very formative experience because it was the first time I had been a bigger part of a big studio movie. We were filming all over the world, so I was wide-eyed the whole time, soaking up as much as I could. It was so interesting to reunite with Ben 11 or 12 years later. I realized how much I had absorbed from that experience. I would see him doing things before takes and recognize little things that I do. I’d think, “Oh, I got that from him.” I didn’t even realize I had picked that up from that time.

John Hamburg, who directed the movie, also made I Love You, Man, which is one of my favorite movies of all time, as well as Along Came Polly. John has had a hand in so many of the comedies that shaped me as a person and an actor. And, of course, you have the greatest actor of all time, literally Robert De Niro. Every day on set was a total out-of-body experience. Then there’s Ariana Grande, one of the most mega-talented performers and artists of all time. She’s been around for what feels like my whole life. It was insane. In some ways, it felt like stepping onto a superhero set, even more so than Superman. You’re shoulder to shoulder with all these total icons, and it was great. It definitely felt like huge shoes to fill. You really felt that sense of responsibility: “Oh my God, I cherish this so much. I don’t want to mess this up.”

We had a great time. Ari is, in addition to everything else, unbelievably funny and an incredible actress. We all knew that, but I hadn’t seen her do something like this. She was brilliantly funny and collaborative. I basically tried to ask Robert De Niro as many questions as possible and spend as much time with him as possible without bothering him. We had a great time.

I’m excited for people to see this movie, and I hope they respond to it. John did a really good job of making this movie feel like it lives in the same world as those first movies, even these many years later.

DEADLINE: And sometime next year, we’ll see you in the Superman film Man of Tomorrow and in The People v. Gorilla Grodd. Under the new James Gunn and Peter Safran DCU regime, you’ll be the first to get your own show: what new aspects of Jimmy Olsen are you excited to explore? And are you nervous about it?

GISONDO: We just wrapped. This is uncharted territory for me, and I don’t know what I can say. I really don’t want to get fired, and I really don’t want to get in trouble [laughs].

GISONDO: I can say American Vandal is one of my favorite shows of all time. Dan Perrault and Tony Yacenda, who were behind that show, are also behind this one. For what this show is, I don’t think there was anybody else in the world who could have done it or who could have done what they would’ve done. I think it’s an approach to this world that I’ve certainly never seen before. People can expect something different. In the movie, Jimmy, because you’re getting a smaller dose of him, it’s [not as big of a performance]. On the show, I can be a little more off-the-wall in some ways. I can take bigger swings.

There’s a difference between being a piece of the puzzle in a very big movie, where you can come in, take really big swings and try things, and being more at the center of a story. It’s a different kind of thing. I think the stakes of the show are surprisingly big. It’s deeply funny, and I think people are going to be surprised by the stakes, where it goes, and how big it gets. It’s definitely a more intense version of Jimmy Olsen than we’ve seen before. I don’t think I can get fired for anything I just said [laughs].

Nat Geo Explorer Bertie Gregory Deeply Cares About the Wolves He Has Followed for Seven Years

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Nat Geo Explorer Bertie Gregory Deeply Cares About the Wolves He Has Followed for Seven Years

In National Geographic’s newest television program, Wolves Up Close With Bertie Gregory, the eponymous British wildlife filmmaker, acclaimed photographer, and ardent environmentalist heads to Hudson Bay, Canada, to film wolves in their harsh Arctic habitat. Gregory chatted with PetaPixel about filming the new documentary.

While the focus of the new documentary is clearly on wolves, a specific wolf pack in particular that Gregory has followed for seven years, Wolves Up Close is a smorgasbord of Arctic wildlife in general. There are polar bears, red foxes, and moose, each beautifully filmed.

“One of the best parts about filming nature, and also the most annoying part, is that it doesn’t read the script,” Gregory laughs. “I always joke with the production team that we often go out with this make-believe script that we’ve written and then nature on day one is like, ‘Nope.’ And that’s the frustrating part, but on the positive part of it, it means that you never know what’s gonna happen.”

In one sequence, a pair of polar bears gets awfully close to Gregory and the rest of his small crew, which is admittedly a bit nerve-wracking for viewers. Gregory brushes off the dangers of wildlife filmmaking, thanks in large part to the local guides he worked with, including Mike Reimer and Albert “Butch” Saunders.

Gregory says he never felt nervous at all about the wolves, which likewise would saunter up to the crew to check things out, but what about the polar bears and moose?

“They’re potentially dangerous animals,” Gregory admits. “But humans are really dangerous animals. You know, statistically much more dangerous than a polar bear or a moose. Just like with humans, you’ve got to read body language to stay safe. But I work with great people, like Butch and Mike, our local guides, and they were really key to keeping everyone safe.”

Besides, Gregory might have been too busy feeling cold to feel fear.

“Any time during the episode, I was cold. I was feeling cold.”

The veteran filmmaker didn’t just experience physical closeness to some of his subjects — again, he’s a trained professional — but also emotional closeness, especially to the wolves. Bertie has followed this same pack of wolves for seven years, and over that time, he argues it’s impossible not to feel a bond.

In contrast to old-school wildlife documentary practices, which effectively demand that filmmakers remain entirely detached from the nature they experience and shoot, Gregory doesn’t fight the natural urge to feel something about his subjects.

“I mean, we’re trained not to,” he admits. “We’re told not to get attached because you can’t get involved, but I think that’s BS. I think there’s no way you can follow a pack of animals, an animal family, for seven years and not care about them.

I think if you don’t care about them, you’re pretty weird.

“I think if you don’t care about them, you’re pretty weird.”

Gregory says he isn’t actively trying to forge any bond, something he is fully aware would be a one-way street since the wolves “don’t care about us,” but he says, “I care about them.”

“There are two strategies when filming animals. You can either hide from the animals and they don’t know you’re there, or in the case of these wolves, you can have them know you’re there and be accepting of your presence. That’s an amazing relationship to have. They see us as kind of a part of the landscape.”

Viewers can truly feel through the screen how much Gregory cares about and loves these wolves. It shapes the way he films them, which I think is undeniably a good thing.

“You know when someone makes you like a bowl or a pot, or when you’ve got kids, they draw you a picture? There’s something intangible about the quality of the work and how much you like it when you know it’s been made with love,” Gregory explains.

There’s something intangible about the quality of the work and how much you like it when you know it’s been made with love.

He believes, rightly, that there’s something special too about wildlife documentaries that have been crafted with “real immense care and admiration for the animal.”

“I don’t think you need to try too hard to illustrate that. It just comes through naturally.”

For Gregory, wolves are easy to care about. He can’t help but wonder why such beautiful, intelligent creatures that are so much like the pet dogs many people love like they’re members of the family are treated so poorly and feared excessively.

As an example, the federal government is weighing a move right now to delist gray wolves and Mexican wolves from the Endangered Species Act, potentially removing the very protections that helped wolves rebound in the United States.

“That’s just so dumb on so many levels,” Gregory says. “So if you care about wolves, make your voice heard. Tell politicians, tell people with influence, tell them to shout about this stuff because it’s important. Not only are wolves important for wild places, but they’re representative of who we are as a species.

So if you care about wolves, make your voice heard.

“It’s so weird that we value their closest living relative, the domestic dog, in such high regard that we let them come into our houses and sleep in our beds, but yet we’re so horrible to the wild version of them,” Gregory says somberly.

On a much more positive note, Gregory and his crew got to experience something extremely special out in the field, filming a pack of wolves chasing down a moose from their drone. It’s moments like these that inspire wildlife filmmakers like Bertie Gregory.

National Geographic graciously shared a clip from the incredible sequence with PetaPixel.

“I realized I was seeing something very special and something that no human in that area had ever seen before,” Gregory says. “Mike says on camera, he’s been watching wolves for 30 years and never seen that.

“We combined amazing traditional knowledge with new technology. That’s the dream combo. Old school with new school, working together.

“I knew we were seeing something special.”

To make it even more special, it was a wolf Gregory has watched grow into a leader over the years, named Kona by “Butch” Saunders, that tried to take down the moose, latching onto one of its rear legs.

“I was like, ‘No one’s ever gonna believe this.’ It’s too good of a story,” Gregory says. “Then it dawned on me that I didn’t think Kona was going to survive it. The toll on his body. It just goes to show how tough these animals are.”

Kona survived the encounter, but there’s a poignant moment during the show when Gregory looks at the aging wolf and reflects on his age. Life in the Arctic is hard, and before you know it, the long, challenging winters have flown by.

Kona may not have many winters left, certainly not as the leader of the pack. But for a beautiful moment, Kona looked every bit the apex predator.

Wolves Up Close With Bertie Gregory premieres tomorrow, November 6, on National Geographic and will stream the following day on Hulu and Disney+.

Kristen Bell Gives Sneak Peek at What's Thawing for Frozen 3

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Kristen Bell shared an update on Frozen 3 after reprising her role as Princess Anna in the upcoming Walt Disney Animation Studios sequel.

Kristen Bell is sharing a glimpse into the unknown.

As production for Frozen 3 heats up, the Nobody Wants This actress detailed stepping back into Princess Anna’s shoes for the third installment in the beloved Disney animated film franchise.

“Honestly, I’m living my dreams. I'm really living my dreams right now,” Bell shared during an appearance on the Nov. 4 episode of The Jennifer Hudson Show. “At 46 years old, you can still live all of your dreams.”

And she also opened the door into singing what will surely be magical musical numbers.

“I recorded two songs on Friday of this past week,” Bell revealed to Jennifer Hudson. “I love them so much.”

Though fans will have to try to let go of any impatience since the third film won’t be hitting theaters until Nov. 24, 2027, the Veronica Mars alum looks forward to the finished product.

“I just am very proud of what's being written,” Bell shared, “and I'm very excited to share it with the world.”

And while it may seem like this is the first time in forever since there has been an update on Frozen 3, Bell and her costar Idina Menzel—who voices Elsa—recently shared how they made sure magic would flow once again for a third film immediately after Frozen 2 was released in 2019.

“The second [Frozen 2] was over I was waiting by the phone and/or texting everyone, ‘When are we gonna do it?’” Bell told E! News in August. “And so I was in an anticipatory place.”

But Menzel emphasized that the Mouse House is always intentional with its moves and will “wait for the right time.”

“They're super patient with it,” she shared. “I guess we were a little impatient because we really like to get back to the process. We love being together. We love revisiting these characters.”

As for what more fans can expect when it’s time to build a snowman once more? Disney Animation head Jared Bush proved that some things may never truly change as Frozen 3 will tell “a very special story” like its predecessors.

That said, he teased to Collider in an interview published Nov. 3 the tale is “one that will surprise people.”

Until Frozen 3 soars into theaters next year, don’t hold your excitement back anymore and read for Frozen secrets…

1. An adaptation of Hans Christian Andersen's 1845 fairy tale The Snow Queen, Frozen was actually in development at Disney for almost a decade (and was briefly considered for a hand-drawn animated feature back in 1937).

The first modern attempt happened in 2002, and when 2003 also proved unsuccessful, more stalled attempts followed over the years. Frozen really didn't start coming together until Christopher Buck, who directed Tarzan, came aboard in 2008, but the film wasn't commissioned until 2011 following Tangled's box office success.

And in 2012, Jennifer Lee, who initially came on as a screenwriter, was named co-director, making her the first woman to direct an animated film by Walt Disney Animation Studios.

2. The movie was going to be titled The Snow Queen after the original fairy tale, but the filmmakers decided to change it to Frozen "because, to us, it represents the movie. Frozen plays on the level of ice and snow but also the frozen relationship, the frozen heart that has to be thawed," producer Peter Del Vecho explained to Bleeding Cool, denying it was inspired by Tangled. (The Snow Queen title was still used overseas.)

3. Idina Menzel and Kristen Bell auditioned and met with the producers of Tangled for the role of Rapunzel, which ultimately went to Mandy Moore. (Santino Fontana, who gave life to Anna's temporary fiancé Hans, originally auditioned for the role of Flynn in the 2010 film as well).

Bell, whose dream since she was a little girl was to voice a Disney character, was the "very first actress" to audition for Anna, with Lee saying, "We did audition a lot of people for that but she was the first and the best. We just fell in love with her."

4. Menzel's audition for Elsa, meanwhile, was actually a table-read with Bell before both had been officially cast, according to Lee.

"They read the whole script and at the end—we didn't have any songs written yet—and what they did was sing a duet and they sung 'Wind Beneath My Wings,' but they sung it like sisters and what you mean to me," she explained. "And there wasn't a dry eye in the house after they sang...that is when they knew, that was the whole potential and understanding the power of music and of what this emotional story can be."

5. In the early versions of the project, Elsa was actually the villain of the story (with one of the early concept art designs being inspired by Bette Midler and her "showgirl stage presence"), according to former Disney animation designer Claire Keane, who released the image on her web site.

"Originally, she was much more villainous," Buck allowed to Nerdist. "It's very easy to go in that direction for that type of character, but as the time went and we evolved the story, she became much more three-dimensional. Before, I thought she was one-dimensional but a very fully fleshed out character."

The moment the character really changed, though, was after they watched earlier cuts and realized it wasn't exactly working: "Someone came up with the brilliant idea...'what if the protagonist, Anna, and Elsa were related?'" art director Michael Giaimo revealed. "Because they weren't always siblings, they weren't always related, and that was the beginning of finding these characters."

6. The hardest character for the Frozen team to nail down aside from Elsa was actually Olaf, who was originally then-villainous Elsa's first guard.

"She created these snowman guards and Marshmallow was kind of a holdover from that because he was this scary snowman guard. Olaf was the very first guard she created," Buck explained, adding he was still funny at that time.

After changing the character of Elsa completely, it wasn't until the directors first sat down with Josh Gad that they found the character. "Josh adlibbed the line 'it's like a baby unicorn' and there was something about that innocence," Del Vecho told Kidz World.

7. One main character who wasn't originally in the movie was Hans, Anna's suitor-turned-villain, with Giaimo explaining, "Hans was never originally in the story in older versions...Kristoff always was in the story or a version of the Kristoff character and Anna. And then Hans was brought in at one point but he wasn't always a villain who turned initially, and then someone came up with the idea of him turning. He was always potentially a villain, but we gave it away much sooner."

8. Anna's oft-repeated "Wait what?" catchphrase? An ad-lib by Bell. She also slipped in one slightly off-color joke. "We slid it under the radar," she detailed to Vanity Fair of Anna's singing, "For years I've roamed these empty halls / Why have a ballroom with no balls?" The NSFW lyric almost got snipped, she admitted, "But then we were like, 'What are you talking about? That’s not what it means. Don’t be a perv.'"

11. After having a few of the first songs they originally composed for the film not make it in, songwriting couple Robert Lopez and Kristen Anderson-Lopez were feeling a little low, so they went for walk in Brooklyn's Prospect Park.

Listening to songs by Adele and Avril Lavigne and being surrounded by nature, Anderson-Lopez told Playbill, "We were in this kind of emo place of imagining what it would be like to be this perfect person who messed up once, and was chased away from everything she has known and all the people she has been sacrificing for."

Within a day, they would write the film's most iconic song that would go on to sell over 10 million copies in 2014 and win the Oscar for Best Original Song. All together now, "Let it go, let it goooooooo."

12. And after hearing "Let It Go" for the first time, Lee said she knew they had to rework the character of Elsa and her motivations completely.

"Half of us were crying. And then I just went, 'I have to rewrite the whole movie,'" she said on the Scriptnotes podcast. "I really, it was—I was just like I'm going to go lie down for a couple minutes. But it was the best thing. We knew we had the movie."

13. On the same podcast, Lee admitted there was "a lot of push and pull" when it came to Elsa's look after her transformation via "Let It Go" and how sexy to make her after this awakening.

"Everyone was seduced by her. And so there was this tug of war I think, a bit, of letting people have a little—people who wanted to have that a little and not be afraid of it, but not make it a sexual statement," the co-director explained. "It's more a moment of, for me, it was like you strut and you say nobody is looking, this is what I'm going to—I'm not going to be afraid of my sexuality. I'm not going to be afraid of who I am. I'm not going to be afraid of anything about myself."

14. After eagle-eyed fans spotted one of many easter eggs in the film—Tangled's Rapunzel (post haircut) and Eugene briefly appearing in a scene during the morning of Elsa's coronation—a compelling fan theory that tied together Frozen, Tangled and Little Mermaid surfaced: Anna and Elsa's parents died when their boat sank...what if it was when they were on their way to the Tangled pair's wedding? And what if the sunken ship in The Little Mermaid is the ship they were sailing on?!

15. This theory only works if you believe Anna and Elsa's parents actually did die in the shipwreck, something Buck speculated about in a Reddit AMA, saying, in his mind, the King and Queen of Arendelle, as well as their "baby boy," did not actually die after being shipwrecked. (Who knew they had a son?)

He said they washed up on the shore of a jungle and built "a tree house" before being "eaten by a leopard." That baby boy? Tarzan, of course.

"That's my fun little world," he later told MTV. While the theory isn't official (and definitely takes a hit when you factor in the events of Frozen II), Buck said, "Whatever people want to believe, go for it. If you want to tie them all together, then do it."

16. The original ending of the film was drastically different, with the earlier versions, of course, presenting Elsa as the villain and not having Anna and Elsa related.

Beginning with a prophecy about "a ruler with a frozen heart will bring destruction to the kingdom of Arendelle," viewers would learn Elsa became the Snow Queen after she was left at the altar and decided to freeze her own heart, according to EW.

By the end of the film, Anna convinces Elsa to use her powers to save Arendelle from an avalanche started by Hans, with the ultimate reveal being the prophecy was actually about the two-faced prince. (Elsa's heart would then unfreeze, making her capable of love again.)

"The problem was that we felt like we had seen it before," Del Vecho told EW of the original ending. "It wasn't satisfying. We had no emotional connection to Elsa—we didn't care about her because she had spent the whole movie being the villain. We weren't drawn in. The characters weren't relatable."

Warner Bros.' Adaptation Of Fantasy Franchise Lunar Chronicles Gets Update

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Warner Bros. is developing a new animated movie based on a fantasy novel series, and the latest update reveals the studio's bold plans for the future.

Over the years, Warner Bros. has released numerous animated flicks like The Looney Tunes, The Iron Giant, and Teen Titans Go! To the Movies. In recent years, there's been the Lord of the Rings movie The War of the Rohirrim and The Day the Earth Blew Up, along with direct-to-video releases for Scooby-Doo, Tom and Jerry, and DC Comics.

Now Warner Bros. Pictures Animation is working on an adaptation of Melissa Meyer's fantasy/sci-fi book series The Lunar Chronicles. The project was first announced three years ago, and The Wrap now has a majorupdate.

Apparently, the studio has grand plans for the future of The Lunar Chronicles, to the point where executives are reportedly planning for the film to be the first installment in a franchise. This shows that Skydance has faith in the property, even after Paramount acquired Warner Bros.

Noëlle Raffaele, who has worked as a storyboard artist on The Lego Ninjago Movie, Scoob!, Ms. Marvel and The Marvels, is directing The Lunar Chronicles adaptation. Industrial Light & Magic is creating the animation.

The Lunar Chronicles reimagines classic fairytale characters with a futuristic twist. The first book, Cinder, came out in 2012 and tells a Cinderella-like story, but the protagonist is a cyborg. Meyer followed that up with Scarlet (inspired by Little Red Riding Hood), Cress (a new take on Rapunzel), Fairest (about Snow White's Evil Queen), Winter (Snow White), the short story collection Stars Above and the Wires, and the Nerve graphic novels.

Meyer has also written several other series, including Renegades, Fortuna Beach and Gilded, and co-wrote the novels Let It Glow, The Escape Game and The Traitor Game.

Multiple animated films have performed really well this year, with Toy Story 5 currently the third-highest-grossing movie of 2026 at $1.15 billion. The Super Mario Galaxy Movie ranks fifth with $1.01 billion, while Minions & Monsters is ninth with $529 million.

Sign in to claim your place on the leaderboard!

Sign in to claim your place on the leaderboard!

Ice Age: Boiling Point, The Bluey Movie, Pixar's Gatto, Shrek 5, Spider-Man: Beyond the Spider-Verse, The New Simpsons Movie, The Haunting of Hotel Transylvania, and Frozen 3 are all animated movies coming out in 2027. Then 2028 will see the arrivals of Shrek spinoff Donkey and Incredibles 3.

Many of these are based on existing properties, and some, like Shrek and Frozen, lean into the fairy-tale genre, so The Lunar Chronicles is in good company when Warner Bros. releases it in a couple of years from now.

The Lunar Chronicles will be released in theaters on Friday, November 3, 2028.

Matthew Lillard’s Shaggy Return In New Scooby-Doo Series Is 2027’s Most Anticipated Comeback

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Matthew Lillard is officially returning as Shaggy in a new Scooby-Doo series arriving in 2027, marking what may be the most anticipated actor comeback of the year, if not the decade. For 2000s kids, this is nothing short of a dream come true, while also giving a whole new generation the chance to discover why Lillard's portrayal of the iconic character is so beloved.

As a 2000s kid, I grew up with the live-action Scooby-Doo (2002) and Scooby-Doo 2: Monsters Unleashed (2004) movies. In fact, the VHS tapes for these two movies were easily among the most loved and frequently watched in my collection, seeing hundreds, if not thousands, of playbacks over the years.

As a result, Matthew Lillard became the face and voice of "my Shaggy," just as he did for hundreds of thousands of kids worldwide. To this day, it's truly impossible for me to imagine another actor in the role who could even come close.

Teenage Mutant Ninja Turtles' latest series has already delivered one of the coolest redesigns of a classic villain.

Now, 22 years after Monsters Unleashed, Lillard is reprising this legendary role, albeit with a twist. Rather than returning for another live-action project, Lillard will voice Shaggy Rogers in the upcoming series Yokoso, Scooby-Doo!, marking the franchise's first original anime series. And while this isn't quite the R-rated live-action return some fans may have hoped for, I couldn't be more excited.

Yokoso, Scooby-Doo! will be a Japan-set, anime-inspired mystery adventure that pairs Scooby-Doo and Shaggy with several new characters. Notably, in addition to Lillard returning as the voice of Shaggy, longtime Scooby-Doo voice actor Frank Welker will also reprise his role as Scooby-Doo. While the franchise has seen countless animated series reboots over the years, fans can expect Yokoso, Scooby-Doo! to offer something wholly unique.

In addition to marking the franchise's first venture into anime, the series will see classic Scooby-Doo shenanigans collide with Japanese folklore, delivering a completely new setting and adventure unlike anything fans are used to. Produced by Japanese animation studio OLM and distributed by Tubi, Yokoso, Scooby-Doo! has yet to confirm an exact release date but is expected to arrive sometime in 2027, meaning Lillard's return is just around the corner.

While Lillard's Shaggy and Welker's Scooby-Doo will understandably be the stars of the show, they'll also be joined by several new characters, including Yume, a girl with magical powers; Takumi, a gadget whiz; and Scooby-Doo's uncle, Daisuke-Doo (We can only hope that Daisuke-Doo is less nefarious than Scooby's nephew, Scrappy-Doo).

Die-hard Scooby-Doo fans already know what to expect from Lillard's voice acting, as he took over as Shaggy's official animated voice around 2010, after original voice actor Casey Kasem retired from the role. Since then, Lillard has voiced Shaggy in numerous animated films, TV series, and video games. However, Yokoso, Scooby-Doo! will mark his first time venturing into anime as Shaggy Rogers, making this latest return particularly notable.

The kids who grew up watching Matthew Lillard's Scooby-Doo movies are now all grown up, and it feels like the perfect time for the actor and his co-stars, Freddie Prinze Jr., Sarah Michelle Gellar, and Linda Cardellini, to reprise their live-action roles. An R-rated movie bringing back the Mystery Inc. gang for a more mature, all-grown-up adventure could be exactly what longtime fans have been waiting for.

Matthew Lillard is already a horror icon thanks to his roles in some of the most legendary horror franchises of all time, including William Afton in Five Nights at Freddy's and Stu Macher in Scream (1996), one of the original Ghostface killers alongside Billy Loomis. Fans of both franchises would undoubtedly go wild if news broke that Lillard's horror and Scooby-Doo legacies were set to collide, especially if his fellow co-stars returned alongside him.

If you grew up a Scooby-Doo kid, let us know in the comments: Would you also like to see Matthew Lillard return for an R-rated Scooby-Doo movie?

Jennifer Lawrence and Kim Kardashian’s NYC Night Out Adds to Their Quirky 11-Year History

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Jennifer Lawrence and Kim Kardashian enjoyed a night out together in New York City yesterday, beginning the evening in regular attire and then ending it in matching Skims pajamas and robes as they left the Four Seasons hotel. At first glance, the pairing might seem unexpected, but Lawrence’s history with the Kardashian family goes back for over 10 years.

In 2023, Lawrence told Kylie Jenner while interviewing her for Interview magazine that she started following the family’s original reality show Keeping Up With the Kardashians when she was a teenager: “I’ve been watching the show since I was, not to date myself, 14, so I would say I know everything about your family,” she shared.

Her first big public moment with the Kardashian-Jenner family was in 2015. Kardashian’s mother, Kris Jenner, posted a photo of Lawrence in bed with her along with a birthday message.

In November 2017, Lawrence interviewed Kardashian on Jimmy Kimmel Live! while serving as a guest host for Jimmy Kimmel.

Kardashian revealed where they first met. “We were at a hotel in New York, and I don’t know where we were coming from, but it was like, 8, and we were going up to see this Axel Vervoordt suite at the Greenwich [Hotel] and I’m getting in the elevator with Kanye [West] and the doors are about to close and I hear Jennifer screaming, ‘I love your show!’ so loud across the whole lobby,” she recalled.

They also discussed Lawrence having dinner at Kris’s house two weeks before their interview and Kardashian dressing her up.

It marked their first significant public conversation:

Less than a year later, Lawrence quipped during a February 2018 event at The Wing that her friendship with Kardashian doesn’t go both ways. “I don’t know that she’d call me her friend,” Lawrence said. “It’s probably a one-sided friendship.”

She recalled learning she’d interview Kardashian on Kimmel’s show. “When I found out that I was going to be interviewing her, I was like, ‘Oh my God, my whole life has been building up to this moment,’” she said. “I wanted to get as much information as I could. I wanted to do my due diligence on Kim Kardashian. I went over to Kris [Jenner’s] house and had dinner with the whole family.”

Of the family generally, Lawrence said, “They are smart, grounded and normal and funny. They’re very nice people, [they’re] very close, [they have a] loyal bond with one another, which I think is a positive thing to put out into the world. They’ve been absolutely lovely to me.”

Lawrence has remained close to the Kardashian-Jenner family in the years since. (Kris even attended Lawrence’s wedding to Cooke Maroney in October 2019.)

During her 2023 conversation with Kylie, Lawrence even jokingly referred to Kris as her mother while asking a question about Kylie’s relationship with her older sister Kendall Jenner. “You and me are Leos,” she told Kylie. “We’re happy, excited golden retrievers. And Kendall, of course, is a Scorpio like our mother, Kris.” She and Kylie ended their chat by exchanging I love yous.

Last month, Lawrence was mentioned on The Kardashians, with Kardashian revealing the actress had asked for Kourtney Kardashian’s contact information to say sorry for a remark she made during a Vanity Fair interview about Kourtney.

Kardashian said that Lawrence wrote in her apology email, “I just feel like I’m one of your sisters.”

An investigation regarding the Jalen Hurts situation has not begun

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The return of Eagles quarterback Jalen Hurts to the team’s facility on Wednesday is a reminder that plenty of questions remain regarding the decisions made as to his health before flying to London with a blood clot in his leg, playing in London with a blood clot in his leg, and flying back from London with a blood clot in his leg.

A widespread presumption has emerged that the NFL and NFL Players Association have commenced a joint investigation into the care and recommendations Hurts received from team and league doctors. However, the investigation has yet to start.

The statement issued by the league and the NFLPA said only that they are “working together to review the matter.” A formal investigation has not yet begun, of any kind. The union has the right under the Collective Bargaining Agreement to initiate an investigation. That hasn’t happened, either.

If the NFLPA will be working with the NFL in any official investigation, it’s critical that the union insist on a process that will be fair, objective, and transparent. It can’t be a CYA exercise.

Basically, it can’t give off the same vibes that have emanated from the team and the league, to date. The union can’t afford to create the impression that it’s complicit in a coverup. And the process needs to lead to real change, aimed at ensuring that someone disconnected from the team (and possibly from the league, especially as to international games) will be involved in medical decisions as to potentially serious health situations.

The NFL and the NFLPA already employ an unaffiliated doctor when it comes to diagnosing concussions and clearing players to return after suffering concussions. Other health conditions require a similar layer of detachment, in order to ensure that the shared incentives of the team and the player for the player to play will not override the importance of treating the player like a patient.

Levon Hawke to Star With Anthony Hopkins in Indie Drama ‘Twenty-Seven’ (EXCLUSIVE)

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Levon Hawke (“Blink Twice,” “The Crowded Room”) will star opposite Academy Award winner Anthony Hopkins (“The Father”) in the drama “Twenty-Seven.”

In the film, written and directed by Gregory Caruso, Hawke portrays a 27-year-old aspiring screenwriter attempting to manage his relationships and his OCD while struggling to break into the film industry. Hopkins plays his friend and mentor, an 80-year-old television actor-turned-theater professor, who is also fighting Parkinson’s disease. Related Stories Abdul El-Sayed Has Heated Exchange With Sky News Reporter Texas' New Attorney General Nathan Johnson Composed the Music for 67 'Dragon Ball Z' Episodes, Years Before Election Day

“I wrote this character hoping someone like Anthony Hopkins would play him. I never imagined it would actually be Anthony Hopkins,” said Caruso. “He brings a mastery to the role I still can’t fully describe, and Levon meets him in every scene with real vulnerability and intelligence. At its heart, ‘Twenty-Seven’ is about the uncertainty of growing up, feeling stuck between where you are and where you want to be, and the people who help you find your way.”

“Twenty-Seven,” from Caruso’s Bristol Pictures, is currently in production in Los Angeles. David Worthen Brooks (“Misty Green,” “Appendage”) will produce alongside Dustin Cook (“Flock of Four,” “The Frontier”).

Caruso’s crew includes director of photography Matthew Pothier (“Mutt”), production designer Emma Rose Mead (“To Leslie”), costume designer Jenny Eagan (“Knives Out”), hair and makeup leads Mandy Artusato and Gina Marie DeAngelis (“One Battle After Another”), editor Aleshka Ferrero (“Euphoria”) and casting director Vickie Thomas (“Michael,” “Misty Green”).

The project marks the first lead role for Hawke, an actor and composer who is the son of Ethan Hawke and Uma Thurman and the younger brother of actress Maya Hawke. Levon made his feature film debut in Zoë Kravitz’ 2024 thriller “Blink Twice” and appeared in the Apple TV series “The Crowded Room.” He then joined the Peter Dinklage-starring western “The Thicket” and appeared in the indie film “Wildcat,” directed by his father and starring his sister. In 2024, Hawke made his directorial debut with the play “Picture Day,” followed by roles in Josh Safdie’s Oscar-nominated film “Marty Supreme” and Hayley Kiyoko’s coming-of-age movie “Girls Like Girls.” The rising star will next be seen in A24’s “Peaked” and on season two of the acclaimed Prime Video series “Mr. & Mrs. Smith.” Hawke is represented by CAA and HJTH.

Two-time Oscar winner Hopkins’ legendary career is marked by such classics as “The Silence of the Lambs,” “Bram Stoker’s Dracula,” “The Remains of the Day,” “Nixon,” “Amistad” and “Meet Joe Black.” Hopkins recently released his debut album “Life is a Dream” and has several acting projects in the works, including D.J. Caruso’s “A Visit to Grandpa’s,” Richard Eyre’s “Housekeeper” and Guy Ritchie’s “Wife & Dog.” Hopkins is represented by UTA and GGSSC.

Kim Kardashian’s Morning Show Look Is a Vintage Paco Rabanne Pastille Party Dress

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Good morning, Kim Kardashian! Starting off a day of press appointments in New York City, the reality star-turned-actor joined her fellow All’s Fair costars on Good Morning America to promote the legal drama’s second season. And while most of us need a cup of coffee (or two) to get ourselves dressed and out the door, she woke up and slipped straight into a vintage Paco Rabanne party dress.

The look, styled by Law Roach, was an intricate peach gown pulled from the Spring/Summer 1989 couture collection. The top was crafted from hundreds of metal discs, which were stitched together to create a one-shoulder silhouette with a side cutout, making for an asymmetrical, backless piece of armor. From the waist, a long silk-crepe skirt floated to the ankle, where it was met by little heeled sandals with clear PVC straps.

The piece of history was sourced by Maison Vintage Virtuoso, a Paris-based archival studio focused on couture, and was reportedly introduced to Kardashian’s team four months ago. (The same ensemble recently sold for nearly $11,500 on 1st Dibs.)

On GMA, Kardashian shared several candid stories from her time filming All’s Fair, and even revealed that she thought Sarah Paulson’s lower-register on-camera voice was her real voice until just a few weeks ago. Despite this confession, the costars were some of the closest on set, and often made one another break character. “They made Sarah and I one time get up and take a walk around the building because they said we needed to get it together,” the Skims founder told the talk show. “We were laughing so hard.”

Kardashian, who later changed into a sheer knit minidress from Dolce and Gabbana’s Autumn/Winter 1997 collection, has curated a press tour wardrobe full of custom gowns (Ashi Studio), vintage pieces (Todd Oldham), and standout couture looks (Schiaparelli).
