Everything Coming to HBO Max in November 2026

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

HBO Max’s November 2026 lineup is stronger and more hotly anticipated than ever before. This year is quite a step-up with the return of award-winning scripted shows and the streaming premieres of a number of high-profile films. Get ready to return to the indulgent, decadent years of The Gilded Age with its brand-new fourth season, alongside the show’s podcast. For biopic fans, A24’s delightful coming-of-age drama about the early career of celebrated chef Anthony Bourdain is sure to warm your hearts. As will the upcoming documentary about the deep friendship between Colin Farrell and Emma Fogarty, which explores how they have been fighting Fogarty’s lifelong medical condition together.

Also in November, the film adaptation of Enid Blyton's book series The Faraway Tree is getting its streaming debut. And if you want to get started with your holiday watchlist, HBO Max is stacked with a long list of holiday-themed movies and specials this month to put you in the festive mood. Here’s a guide to everything coming to HBO Max in November 2026.

Julian Fellowes’ hit follow-up to Downton Abbey, The Gilded Age is returning for another season of drama and decadence. The HBO original historical drama follows high-society politics, conflicts, and romance in 1800s New York City, where a newly arrived Marian Brook gets entangled in a ruthless social war between her affluent aunts and their wealthy neighbors. Carrie Coon, Christine Baranski, Cynthia Nixon, Louisa Jacobson, and Morgan Spector return as the main characters, with Dennis Haysbert joining the stacked cast as a new character.

Since its first season premiere in 2022, The Gilded Age has become quite popular among Downton Abbey fans, earning praise for its luxurious production, rich costumes, and the sizzling performances of the lead cast. The upcoming fourth season is set between 1885 and 1886 and picks up six months after the sensational finale of Season 3, where Bertha Russell’s rise up the ranks of New York’s high society comes at a heavy price while Agen van Rhijn attempts to regain her social status. Also releasing alongside the show's new chapter is Season 4 of The Gilded Age Podcast, hosted by Alicia Malone and Tom Meyers, where they break down every episode against the real-world history of the era.

A Brazilian crime drama series, City of God: The Fight Rages On is based on the semi-autobiographical novel City of God by Paulo Lins and serves as a follow-up to the 2002 film, City of God. The Fight Rages On is set two decades after the events of the film, following photojournalist Wilson “Rocket” Rodrigues as he documents the conflicts between the drug dealers, police, militiamen, and politicians in the titular area of Rio de Janeiro and their impact on its residents. Directed by Kátia Lund and Fernando Meirelles, the show stars Alexandre Rodrigues, Thiago Martins, Roberta Rodrigues, Sabrina Rosa, Edson Oliveira, Marcos Palmeira, and Andréia Horta.

City of God: The Fight Rages On premiered in 2024 and was well-received by critics, who praised the show’s faithfulness to the original novel and movie. But when compared to its critically acclaimed predecessor, the HBO Max original series has been critiqued for falling short on the visual brilliance and visceral narrative that earned the 2002 film four Academy Award nominations. Nevertheless, the upcoming second season should make for an intriguing watch for crime drama fans.

Directed by Matt Johnson, Tony is the first feature film that chronicles the story of celebrity chef Anthony Bourdain in a fictionalized, coming-of-age comedy narrative. Taken from the chef’s own anecdotes, as detailed in his memoir, Kitchen Confidential, the film follows a 19-year-old Anthony “Tony” Bourdain in 1975 as he experiences a transformative summer in Provincetown, Massachusetts. The film stars Dominic Sessa as Bourdain, with Antonio Banderas, Emilia Jones, Stavros Halkias, and Leo Woodall as other main characters.

A gastronome, writer, storyteller, and television personality, Anthony Bourdain was the voice that defined a generation, inspiring the culinary world (and beyond) like no other with his explorations of culture, cuisine, and the human condition. The A24 biographical drama serves as a heartfelt homage to his genius and a celebration of his dedication to his craft. On its theatrical premiere in August 2026, Tony earned positive reviews from critics and was widely praised for its charming storytelling and Banderas’ deeply felt performance.

Directed by Ben Gregor and written by Simon Farnaby, The Magic Faraway Tree is a film adaptation of Enid Blyton’s The Faraway Tree children’s book series. The plot follows a modern urban family who move to the countryside, where the three young siblings embark on a fantastical adventure into the magical realms of the titular tree, which also brings the family closer than ever. The film boasts a stellar ensemble cast featuring Andrew Garfield, Claire Foy, Nicola Coughlan, Rebecca Ferguson, and Judi Dench in key roles.

Following its UK theatrical premiere in March 2026 and US premiere in August 2026, The Magic Faraway Tree will make its streaming debut on HBO Max on November 27, 2026. On its theatrical release, it became the highest-grossing independent film at the time in the UK, winning the National Film Awards for Best Comedy. Fun, playful, and charming, The Magic Faraway Tree’s whimsical family adventure is sure to uplift your spirits this season.

The Slightest Touch is a documentary film that follows the deep, lifelong friendship between actor Colin Farrell and Emma Fogarty — the oldest woman in Ireland surviving with severe Epidermolysis Bullosa. To celebrate Emma's 40th birthday, Farrell and Fogarty join the Dublin Marathon, where Farrell pushes Emma in a wheelchair for the final stretch. The film is directed by Rachel Fleit, an American director, producer, and writer, who fans might know from her earlier documentaries like Introducing, Selma Blair and Bama Rush.

A celebration of friendship and community, The Slightest Touch chronicles a heartwarming story of friendship, exploring how Farrell’s and her family’s enduring support has helped Fogarty navigate life with a rare, painful genetic condition. The endeavor helped raise donations for DEBRA Ireland Charity to support EB research and patient care and increase awareness of the rare disease. After screening at the Dublin International Film Festival in February 2026 and the London Film Festival in October 2026, The Slightest Touch will make its streaming debut on HBO Max this November.

Stephen King's 1 Main Request For Hit Sci-Fi Adaptation's Return This Month Confirmed

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

EXCLUSIVE: When The Institute returns for its second season this month, it will feature a scene that was personally requested by author Stephen King, according to the show's lead star.

The Institute is based on King's 2019 novel, which tells the story of kids with telekinetic and telepathic powers (including Luke Ellis) who get abducted by a mysterious organization known as the Institute. Six years later, MGM+ and creator/showrunner Benjamin Cavell debuted the TV adaptation, which stars Ben Barnes, Joe Freeman, Simone Miller, Fionn Laird and Mary-Louise Parker.

King serves as one of the executive producers on The Institute alongside Cavell, Jack Bender and others. In an interview with ScreenRant's Grant Hermanns, Barnes, who plays Tim Jamieson, revealed that the bestselling horror author had one request for The Institute season 2. He "very deliberately" wanted to see the children use their powers to "get cash out of an ATM."

King had that visual in his head, which Barnes can relate to because "when you're dreaming and conjuring stories, or writing songs, or anything, you have something very specific in your head."

Sign in to claim your place on the leaderboard!

Sign in to claim your place on the leaderboard!

Cavell already had a connection to King before working on The Institute, having co-created and executive-produced Paramount+'s 2020 adaptation of The Stand, so the author was familiar with Cavell and knew how he worked, especially when it came to bringing his stories to life on the screen.

King was "quite thrilled" with how The Institute turned out, according to Barnes, who explained that Cavell made sure to incorporate certain themes that King typically includes in his books, like "fighting back against the man" and the "powers that be," as well as how innocence can be corrupted and "really ask[ing] difficult questions of people that are in charge."

Ben Barnes: "We didn't particularly have individual connections with Stephen King, but obviously, Ben Cavell, our showrunner, did, and I think he was quite thrilled with how the first season turned out thematically. Certainly, in terms of the thing that you associate with Stephen King so deliberately, his concerns about fighting back against the man, against the powers that be, the corruption of innocence, the power of youth — in this case, the supernatural power of youth — to stand up for itself, and kind of really ask difficult questions of people that are in charge. I think so long as we carried through those themes into the second season, then we were going to be on the right path. I did hear that he very deliberately wanted the kids to use their superpowers to get cash out of an ATM. [Laughs] That was his one visual, in his head, which I connect to because when you're dreaming and conjuring stories, or writing songs, or anything, you have something very specific in your head that you're like, 'Oh, it'd be cool to see that.' I love that he deliberately said, 'I want to see that.'"

Barnes and everyone who works on The Institute knew that if they continued to carry those messages forward into season 2, "then we were going to be on the right path."

The first season earned a 64% critic score and a 69% audience score on Rotten Tomatoes. Now, a little over a year later, the cast and crew are back for the second season, with Game of Thrones Alfie Allen joining the cast as billionaire Nolan Reeves.

After The Institute season 1 finale saw the characters destroy the facility, Luke, Tim and the other survivors are now working towards their next goal: to uncover the crimes committed by the Institute.

King's stories have been adapted numerous times throughout his career, a trend that's not slowing down anytime soon. Recently, there have been movies based on The Monkey, The Long Walk and The Running Man as well as Mike Flanagan's hit new TV show Carrie, which has already been renewed for a second season.

The Institute is also coming back for season 2, as the drama, secrets and conspiracies continue to grow with each episode.

The Institute season 2 premieres Sunday, November 8 at 9 p.m. EST on MGM+.

The Institute, released in 2025, follows 14-year-old genius Luke Ellis, who is kidnapped and wakes up at a mysterious facility filled with children possessing unusual abilities. Meanwhile, Tim Jamieson, a former police officer, seeks a fresh start in a nearby town, inevitably intertwining his fate with Luke's.

Agatha Christie Meets Hallmark In Netflix's New Detective Series Trending At The Perfect Time

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Now that it's November, audiences are ready to snuggle up and dive into new 2026 TV shows, and the series trending on Netflix right now prove that we're ready to stay in. It's no surprise that the best shows on Netflix to watch as the holidays approach have elements of mystery, romance, and a cozy atmosphere that will leave viewers ready to binge every available season. While there are plenty of Netflix originals that scratch this itch, new additions to the streamer's catalog shouldn't be overlooked.

Some of the most popular series on any streaming service are mysteries and thrillers that draw audiences in with their incredible twists and turns. Many of the best mystery TV shows draw inspiration from Agatha Christie books, finding ways to incorporate the drama and intrigue of small-town life into shocking detective work. However, the darkest thrillers aren't always right for the fall and winter months. There are warm, comforting mysteries taking Netflix by storm.

Mistletoe Murders is the Hallmark original series that audiences can't stop watching on Netflix right now. Starring Sarah Drew, known for playing the TV doctor April Kepner on Grey's Anatomy, Mistletoe Murders sees Drew step into a new role as Emily Lane, a woman with a mysterious past who moves to a small town in upstate New York and must balance running her Christmas shop with the criminal investigations that keep pulling her in.

Mistletoe Murders seasons 1 and 2 were added to Netflix on November 1st, and the cozy mystery series has already begun to dominate the charts on the streaming service. As audiences gear up for the holidays, it's the perfect time for Mistletoe Murders to join the Netflix catalog, giving viewers a series that blends mystery with romance and small-town charm, like all the best projects from Hallmark. Drew is joined by co-star Peter Mooney, who plays the detective, Sam Wilner, who isn't too sure about Emily's off-the-books investigations.

Set in the town of Fletcher's Grove, the series follows Emily as she moves in and starts running her Christmas shop, a seemingly normal new resident of the tight-knit community. However, the secrets Emily has been keeping about her past rear their head when murder comes to stay in Fletcher's Grove. It doesn't take long for Sam and Emily to team up and go on some Christmas-themed adventures to prove the innocence of some townspeople and track down the real killers.

One of Netflix's most popular mystery thriller series has officially become the streaming platform's most watched show in the world.

Already in the top ten TV shows streaming on Netflix (via FlixPatrol), Mistletoe Murders is exploding in popularity for good reason. With only 12 total episodes on Netflix, it's hard to stop watching Mistletoe Murders once you've started. From the chemistry between Emily and Sam to the Christmas mysteries that will make viewers feel like the holidays are just around the corner, Mistletoe Murders isn't corny, as some Hallmark shows are criticized for being, but a heartwarming project with a thrilling edge that sets it apart.

Mistletoe Murders season 3 premieres Thursday, November 5th, at 8/7c on Hallmark+. Unfortunately, fans won't be able to watch the next installment of the show on Netflix, but based on the audience that Mistletoe Murders has already found on the streaming service, it wouldn't be surprising to see season 3 of the series make its way to Netflix before too long. In the meantime, new episodes exploring Emily and Sam's next investigations will be available on Hallmark+.

It's hard to believe that Mistletoe Murders premiered in 2024, and we're just now discovering how much fun it is to watch the series. Fortunately, if the success on Netflix is any indicator, there's reason to hope that Mistletoe Murders won't end with season 3, and fans will have even more new seasons to look forward to. While Mistletoe Murders would be fun to watch any time of year thanks to the charming premise and likable cast, it hits even harder this time of year, and we can't wait for November 5th when the series returns.

Louisa Jacobson’s Suited Streetwear Couldn’t Be More Different From Her <i><em>Gilded Age </em></i>Character’s Style

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

From corsets to bustles to floor-length skirts, the womenswear of the Gilded Age was known for many things. But Louisa Jacobson’s frayed raw hems and buckled collars were certainly not in the ladies’ 1880s style guides.

While doing press in New York City for the fourth season of her smash HBO historical drama The Gilded Age, Jacobson showed us a bit of her own street style... and it was a far cry from the looks that her character, Marian Brook, has donned on the very same streets.

Leaning instead into a bit of menswear, rather than the pastel gowns we’ve come to expect from her on-screen alter-ego, the actor experimented with suiting and modernized tailoring, thanks to the help of her stylist, Edward Bowleg III.

Last Monday, Jacobson turned to Mel Usine for a light-gray set from the label’s Spring/Summer 2027 collection. Composed of a frayed, collarless jacket and matching inside-out skirt, the ensemble offered an unfinished, deconstructed look you’d have never seen during the 1883 Metropolitan Opera House opening.

It was layered over a black-trimmed white tank with a skinny red tie wrapped around her waist, and paired with peep-toe heels.

The next day, the actor pushed things even further, wearing a black-on-black monochrome look from Commission’s Spring/Summer 2027 collection. This one was all about the buckle accents, with a zip-up blazer topped with a belted collar and little capri leggings trimmed with the same detail. In between was a pleated skirt, and for shoes, Jacobson wore pointed-toe pumps over calf-high socks.

Later in the day, Jacobson was a proper Calvin Klein girl—seen in Hudson Square wearing a dark-clay satin blazer and matching trousers with a heather-gray jacket over both. Her little nude ballet flats featured a contrasting reflective strap, and she grabbed a shoulder bag before heading out for an afternoon of press.

In an age of constant method dressing, it appears Jacobson is doing things her way.

Joe Fagnano will be the Ravens QB on Thursday night

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Ravens quarterback Joe Fagnano practiced on Tuesday and he’s set to play on Thursday.

Head coach Jesse Minter told reporters after Tuesday’s practice that Fagnano will start against the Jaguars. Fagnano practiced with a heavy brace on his right ankle, but is healthy enough to make his first NFL start.

Fagnano will be the second undrafted rookie to start a game in Ravens history. Anthony Brown was the first during the 2022 season.

Fagnano made his first regular season appearance in Sunday’s loss to the Bills after injuries to Lamar Jackson and Tyler Huntley. He was 10-of-21 for 91 yards and an interception.

The injuries led the Ravens to sign Kedon Slovis to the active roster and Austin Reed to the practice squad over the last couple of days. If Fagnano has to leave the game, one of them is in line to become the next man up at quarterback in Baltimore.

7 Upcoming Fantasy Shows, Ranked by Hype

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

2026 has been a pretty solid year for fantasy television. So far, the year has seen new seasons of shows like House of the Dragon and Interview with the Vampire, as well as brand-new series like Avatar: Seven Havens and Witch Hat Atelier. However, as fun as those shows are, there are several exciting fantasy shows in the pipeline right now that could potentially blow those hits out of the water.

With release dates across the rest of 2026, 2027, and beyond, several upcoming shows have garnered sky-high hype among fantasy fans, drumming up lots of conversations ahead of their premieres. Not all of the conversations are positive, of course, and there’s at least one upcoming project that’s been talked about for all the wrong reasons, but on the whole, the future of fantasy television does look pretty bright. Without further ado, here are some of the most fascinating upcoming fantasy shows ranked by hype.

Based on J. K. Rowling’s bestselling fantasy books, Harry Potter is an upcoming fantasy series that’s the second adaptation of the novels after the film series. Starring Dominic McLaughlin as Harry, Alastair Stout as Ron Weasley, and Arabella Stanton as Hermione Granger, the show’s first season adapts the first book, Harry Potter and the Philosopher's Stone, and details Harry’s discovery of the wizarding world and his first year at Hogwarts School of Witchcraft and Wizardry. The ensemble cast also includes John Lithgow as Albus Dumbledore, Janet McTeer as Minerva McGonagall, Paapa Essiedu as Severus Snape, and Nick Frost as Rubeus Hagrid.

HBO has big plans for Harry Potter, with early reports estimating that the new show could span a decade of production. The series has already been renewed for a second season as well. However, the whole project has been largely met with the opposite of hype. Comparisons to the movies (the last of which came out in 2011), negative public opinion of J.K. Rowling, and the idea that the whole project is a naked cash grab have all led to a generally dismal reception from the core fanbase ahead of the show’s release.

Created by Richard Garfield, Magic: The Gathering is one of the most successful game franchises of all time. A trading card game that’s expanded to various other media, the franchise is set across multiple realities and centered on Planeswalkers, dueling wizards with the ability to cross between worlds. While the game has been adapted as comics and novels before, it’s yet to get a movie or TV adaptation.

The Magic: The Gathering series adaptation has been in development since 2019 and has undergone many behind-the-scenes changes, but it finally looks like the show might actually make it to screens. The new show will be a Netflix animated series with Terry Matalas (12 Monkeys) serving as showrunner. The series was previewed at the 2025 Annecy International Animation Film Festival, but it doesn’t yet have a release date. Nonetheless, fans of the franchise are certainly excited for the show’s release and for the upcoming movie adaptation directed by Matt Johnson (Tony).

One of the bestselling romantasy books of the 2020s, Rebecca Yarros’s Fourth Wing is the first book of the Empyrean new adult fantasy romance series. A series adaptation of the books has been in the works at Prime Video since 2023, with Yarros serving as an executive producer on the project alongside Michael B. Jordan, Lisa Joy, Jonathan Nolan, Liz Pelletier, Athena Wickham, and showrunner Meredith Averill. The show, like the book, follows the story of Violet Sorrengail, who joins the Basgiath War College and trains to become a dragon rider, encountering deadly quests, gruelling competition, and unexpected romance. Esther McGregor will star as Violet, with Emilio Sakraya as her rival/love interest, Xaden Riorson.

Fourth Wing is one of the biggest BookTok hits of the romantasy genre, so its series adaptation has a lot of people very excited, and with good reason. Featuring fast-paced action, high-tension romance, and, of course, dragons, Fourth Wing has all the ingredients to become a fantasy TV landmark to rival Game of Thrones. Whether it will actually achieve that potential remains to be seen.

Four worlds and four completely different ideas of what a good life looks like. This is where you belong, what it would ask of you, and what you would get back in return.

You belong somewhere old enough that the mountains have names and somebody can still tell you who gave them. Middle-earth is for people who think the right thing is worth doing even when it will not be finished in their lifetime.

You want the sea, a crew, and absolutely nobody telling you where you are allowed to go. The Grand Line is the world for people whose main ambition in life is finding out what is over there.

You would rather be feared and useful than liked and useless. The Continent is a world where the monsters are the straightforward part of the job and the people are where it all gets complicated.

You want the myths to be real and loose in the actual world, and you want to find out at about thirteen that you were never anywhere near as ordinary as everybody kept telling you.

Mike Flanagan’s The Dark Tower is an upcoming multi-season series adaptation of the fantasy book series by Stephen King. Published between 1982 and 2012, the books revolve around Roland Deschain, a gunslinger on an unending quest across a surreal landscape in search of the titular tower, which sits at the center of a vast multiverse. The books were previously adapted as a 2017 film, which was widely panned by critics, but the series is a complete reboot of the story, and it’s been reported that it will star Sam Strike and Jasper Pääkkönen.

Spanning eight connected novels, one novella, a children’s book, and connections to almost all of King’s celebrated novels, The Dark Tower is the holy grail of Stephen King adaptations. It’s easy to see why the upcoming series has so many people excited, even though it doesn’t have a release date or any concrete details as yet. What little we know about The Dark Tower so far suggests an in-depth adaptation that could prove to be Flanagan’s most ambitious project yet and one of the most fascinating fantasy shows ever made. Or, like the movie, it could just be an expensive failure; only time will tell.

It’s no exaggeration to say that Brandon Sanderson is one of the biggest names in fantasy literature today, and The Stormlight Archive novel series, part of Sanderson’s Cosmere universe, is one of the author’s most popular hits. Currently comprising five novels and two novellas (with plans for a total of 10 novels), it’s an expansive saga set on the battered world of Roshar, which is threatened by magical storms, political tensions, and a looming cataclysm. The books have already inspired a VR experience and a tabletop game, and will soon become an Apple TV series.

As part of Sanderson’s deal with the streaming platform, the author will be personally leading adaptations of his Mistborn novels and The Stormlight Archive, the first as a movie and the second as a TV show, the first season of which will consist of 10 episodes. It’s still early days — the project doesn’t even have a showrunner yet — but it’s easily one of the most hotly anticipated fantasy shows currently in development. With complex character dynamics, intricate worldbuilding, and a fascinating magic system, The Stormlight Archive series has all the potential to become one of the biggest fantasy shows of all time, though, of course, right now, that’s all just speculation.

Developed by Sony Pictures Television, Amazon MGM Studios, and PlayStation Productions, Prime Video’s God of War is the first series adaptation of the video game franchise. Based on the franchise’s Norse mythology era, the show follows Kratos and his son Atreus on a journey to the highest peak of the nine realms, where they hope to spread the ashes of their wife and mother, Faye. Ronald D. Moore serves as the showrunner, and the series stars Dave Bautista and Callum Vinson as Kratos and Atreus, respectively.

God of War fans have been waiting for a live-action adaptation for a long time, and the series is quite highly anticipated as a result. The production has had some significant delays, with original Kratos actor Ryan Hurst suffering an injury during filming, which has prompted a recasting and reshoots. We don’t know when the show will finally arrive, but Prime Video has given it a two-season order, and production is currently expected to end sometime in October 2027.

The second anime adaptation of Eiichiro Oda’s One Piece, The One Piece is a new original net animation show produced by Studio Wit and set to release on Netflix in February 2027. The series is a fresh reboot of the franchise adapted directly from the manga (not the iconic 1999 anime), and it will follow the adventures of Monkey D. Luffy (Mayumi Tanaka), an aspiring pirate who sets out in search of the titular treasure alongside his motley crew, the Straw Hats. Directed by Masashi Koizuka and written by Taku Kishimoto, with character designs by Kyoji Asano and Takatoshi Honda, the show’s seven-episode first season will adapt the first 50 chapters of the manga.

One Piece is one of those anime/manga franchises that’s so big it’s recognizable even to people who’ve never seen an anime before, so it’s pretty understandable why Wit’s The One Piece is so highly anticipated. The new adaptation is expected to fix the classic anime’s pacing issues and elevate the animation style, giving the manga the fully modernized adaptation it deserves and bringing a new generation of viewers to the franchise.

Ben Barnes Officially Addresses Netflix's Narnia Reboot & Magician's Nephew Start

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

EXCLUSIVE: Ben Barnes already has some very glowing thoughts on Narnia: The Magician's Nephew.

Barnes memorably got his first major start in the world of acting by starring as Prince Caspian in the aptly-named adaptation of C.S. Lewis' 1951 Chronicles of Narnia sequel, in which he and the Pevensie siblings sought to reclaim the throne of King from his corrupt uncle. He later returned for 2010's The Voyage of the Dawn Treader, in which Caspian, Edmund, Lucy and their cousin Eustace journeyed to save seven lost lords from a mysterious evil entity.

After years of attempts to get a fourth film off the ground, the rights for Lewis' franchise eventually returned to his estate and were acquired by Netflix, with Greta Gerwig inking a two-movie deal beginning with Narnia: The Magician's Nephew. Set for a February 2027 release, the new film starts by adapting the first chronological novel, in which young friends Digory and Polly find themselves pulled into an adventure through the multiverse by the former's uncle.

On the heels of the first trailer's record-setting release, ScreenRant's Grant Hermanns interviewed Ben Barnes for The Institute season 2, during which the conversation shifted to asking about Netflix's Narnia reboot. The former franchise star beamed as he expressed that "the trailer looks great," and that he is "always a fan of directors who are real visionaries" and who have "clear ideas about what they want to do."

Barnes went on to praise Gerwig's decision to start with The Magician's Nephew for her Narnia franchise, calling it a "brilliant idea," and expressing his excitement about the cast she's put together and seeing "what she will do going forward":

Ben Barnes: I think she's got a great eye for casting. I loved Emma Mackey in Sex Education, I thought she was brilliant. Obviously, Daniel Craig's brilliant in everything. So, yeah, I'm really excited to see it. Those stories are beautiful, but also, every generation needs to reinterpret things, and pull out of stories what seems visceral and important, and present it in a way that is visually exciting, and I think we can rely on her to do that.

Prior to the rights expiring and reverting to Lewis' estate, Walden Media had plans for a fourth Chronicles of Narnia movie adapting the fourth novel, The Silver Chair, with Life of Pi's David Magee set to pen the script for the sequel. However, no casting or directing details were ever made clear about the film, namely whether Will Poulter would reprise his role as Eustace, or Barnes as Caspian, given the novel's Narnia story takes place 70 years after The Voyage of the Dawn Treader and has the character as an older man.

Ever since the film's initial announcement, Barnes and the rest of his fellow Chronicles of Narnia co-stars have frequently been asked their thoughts on Netflix's Magician's Nephew reboot, all of which being that of support for the film. In particular, the group all stated their confidence and blessing in Gerwig's vision for the film, with original Susan actor Anna Popplewell recently sharing at Comic Con Brussels that she was thrilled by the different look and style the Oscar winner showcased in the first trailer.

Interestingly, one star who only just learned about Narnia: The Magician's Nephew was that of Liam Neeson, voice of Aslan in the Walden Media-produced original trilogy. Presented with the information during a recent interview with ScreenRant, the Oscar nominee expressed surprise at the new film's impending release, though chuckled as he stated that "if you're going to make Christ a lady, nobody better than Meryl [Streep]."

Plus, with Netflix setting the film as its first true venture into wide theatrical releases and major IMAX showings, the new reboot is certainly shaping up to be as bold and large-scale as its predecessors.

Adapted from the sixth book in C.S. Lewis' The Chronicles of Narnia saga, The Magician's Nephew follows the universe-hopping misadventures of Digory Kirke and Polly Plummer as children, long before the events of The Lion, the Witch and the Wardrobe.

Oscar Predictions: Animated Feature — A New and Competitive Era for the Medium That Isn’t Just for Kids

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Variety Awards Circuit section is the home for all awards news and related content throughout the year, featuring the following: the official predictions for the upcoming Oscars, Emmys, Grammys and Tony Awards ceremonies, curated by Variety chief awards editor Clayton Davis. The prediction pages reflect the current standings in the race and do not reflect personal preferences for any individual contender. As other formal (and informal) polls suggest, competitions are fluid and subject to change in response to buzz and events. Predictions are updated every Thursday. Related Stories Law Roach Wants to Try Acting: ‘If I’m Terrible at It, at Least I Tried’ 'I Play Rocky' Star Anthony Ippolito on Becoming Sylvester Stallone and Acting With a Dog: 'He Wasn't a Huge Fan of Me'

Best Animated Feature Predictions Commentary (Updated: Nov. 3, 2026): This year, animated features aren’t just for kids. And that’s a good thing.

Laika’s “Wildwood” is the company’s first PG-13 film in its 17 years as a feature studio. At 130 minutes, it ties the record for the longest English-language animated film ever made. Director and producer Travis Knight is already an Oscar nominee for “Kubo and the Two Strings” and has earned considerable respect from his peers and audiences alike. “Wildwood” is among the year’s most acclaimed films and, despite a less-than-stellar box office performance, currently looks like the one to beat.

Knight was among the filmmakers featured in this year’s Pixels and Pencils panel, Variety’s conversation with animation directors at the SCAD Savannah Film Festival. He was joined by Brad Bird (“Ray Gunn”), Daniel Chong (“Hoppers”), Joel Crawford and Januel Mercado (“Forgotten Island”) and Leah Nelson (“Tangles”), all of whom are also in the animated feature race.

And that’s just the group represented on the panel. Louis Clichy’s “Iron Boy” and Andrew Stanton’s “Toy Story 5” are among the other contenders helping make this one of the category’s most competitive lineups in recent years.

Since “Guillermo del Toro’s Pinocchio” won the animated feature Oscar at the 95th Academy Awards, neither Disney nor Pixar has won the trophy. Instead, “The Boy and the Heron,” “Flow” and “KPop Demon Hunters” have taken home the prize, marking a significant shift for a category largely dominated by those two studios since “Shrek” won the inaugural award in 2002.

Then there’s “Tangles,” a film that makes no attempt to disguise its ambitions as an adult story. First-time feature director Leah Nelson co-wrote the film with Sarah Leavitt, adapting Leavitt’s graphic memoir about her mother’s battle with Alzheimer’s disease in the late 1990s. Abbi Jacobson voices Sarah, while Julia Louis-Dreyfus plays her mother. Louis-Dreyfus is also a producer, alongside Seth Rogen and Lauren Miller Rogen.

Both “Tangles” and “Wildwood” are also being submitted in the drama categories at the Golden Globes, where animated films have traditionally defaulted to comedy. It’s technically a matter of category placement, but it also signals something about how these creative teams want their films to be perceived.Of course, none of this is entirely new. “Persepolis” and “Anomalisa” earned Oscar nominations years ago, and Japanese filmmakers have been making animation for adults for as long as the medium has existed.

Only three animated films — “Beauty and the Beast,” “Up” and “Toy Story 3″ — have ever earned best picture nominations, and the last of those came 15 years ago. In a year where we could find our first documentary feature nominated for best picture (“You Can See Everything”), could an animated film join the lineup, too?

The list of predictions in all categories can be found here.

The Governors Awards, which will hand out honorary Oscars to Glenn Close and Ridley Scott, will take place Sunday, Nov. 15. Preliminary voting for the Oscars shortlist in 12 categories runs Dec. 7-11, with the shortlist announcement to follow Tuesday, Dec. 15. Following the close of the eligibility period on Dec. 31, 2026, nominations voting will run Jan. 11-15, 2027. The official Oscar nominations will be announced on Thursday, Jan. 21, 2027. The 99th Oscars will take place on Sunday, March 14, 2027 and will be hosted by Conan O’Brien. The ceremony airs live at 7 p.m. ET on ABC and in more than 200 territories worldwide from the Dolby Theatre at Ovation Hollywood.

Sione Vaki emerges as Lions' change-of-pace back to pair with Jahmyr Gibbs

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Even though the Lions have only played seven games and most teams have played eight, Lions running back Jahmyr Gibbs is leading the NFL with 191 touches — a huge workload that raises questions about whether Detroit needs a player to share carries with Gibbs, the way David Montgomery had done before he was traded to Houston.

Vaki had a career-high 11 carries and a career-high 65 yards in Sunday’s win over the Vikings, and his direct running style was a valuable change of pace for the Lions to have while Gibbs got some breathers between his own 23 carries for 153 yards.

Lions coach Dan Campbell emphasized after the game that the Lions told Vaki they needed him to step up against the Vikings, and Vaki answered the bell.

“Vaki was one of the guys we challenged this week going into this game,” Campbell said. “We were going to need him. He needed to show up. He was going to need to get some carries for us, some significant carries. They needed to be real yards, and he was going to need to be able to pass protect for us. And I thought he took a big step today. That’s a huge step. He gained a lot of trust today, a ton.”

Lions quarterback Jared Goff said the entire offense is excited about what Vaki can bring.

“Vaki ran his tail off,” Goff said. “And when he’s able to do that, it just gives Gibbs that much more of a breath on the sideline to come back and do his thing at his full capacity. And the O-line loved it. They loved the challenge of, like, we’re going to keep leaning on you guys. And I thought they played great.”

The Lions think they have the best running back in the league in Gibbs. If Vaki can prove to be one of the better backup running backs in the league, the Lions’ running game will be in very good shape.

12 Gifts for the Outdoorsy Person Who Already Has All the Gear

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

If you buy through our links, we may earn an affiliate commission. This supports our mission to get more people active and outside.Learn about Outside Online's affiliate link policy

Black Diamond RockLock Magnetron Carabiner

Dale of Norway 1994 Lillehammer Wool Sweater

Camp Snap CS8 Point and Shoot Video Camcorder

Some might argue that the person who already has everything is the hardest to shop for. But we disagree. It’s an opportunity to throw practicality out the window and find something so offbeat, clever, or downright whimsical that they’d never think to buy it themselves. That’s what makes shopping for them so fun. Consider it your chance to give something unexpected, such as the 12 gifts below.

They might have all the gear, but have they ever made their own gear? When the weather is crappy and the daylight wanes, this DIY zipper pouch is just the thing to keep them busy. Ripstop by the Roll provides the Dyneema fabric, YKK coil zipper and slider, and drawcord (thread not included), and it’s up to them to put everything together. Choose from three sizes.

For the nature lover who always points out the tiniest alpine flowers on hikes, this pocket-sized notebook for pressing botanical treasures is the perfect stocking stuffer. The cream-colored pages fold accordion-style for a double layer so moisture doesn’t leak through, and a tight elastic band wraps around the whole journal for secure compression.

Who knew a triangle of fabric could be so versatile? Made from Polartec Alpha microfleece, this scarf is cozy enough to boost warmth but slim enough to slide under layers on the coldest days. It’s wide enough to wrap multiple times around the neck, or once around the shoulders or head. The edges are bound in one of Skida’s signature patterns—leopard print or florals—for a subtle boost of personality.

It’s back! After a six-year hiatus, your climbing-obsessed loved one will be delighted to know this beloved magnetic locking carabiner is in production again. Unlike locking carabiners that require users to twist a screw tight, the Magnetron locks automatically when the gate is closed. The first rerun sold out, but restocks arrived just in time for gifting season.

Published in 2025, this gorgeous coffee table book presents magazines, catalogs, and other printed materials from the past 100 years of the outdoor industry. Iconic brands featured include Snow Peak, The North Face, and Columbia, with a foreword from photographer Chris Burkard. Anyone who’s a nerd about gear will leave this book out, flip through its 384 pages obsessively, and return to it for endless inspiration.

The Hex 2.0 is a chair, a stadium seat, a sleeping pad, and a kneeling mat, all in one. With both straps clipped together to create a supportive seat, the eight-millimeter high-density cored closed-cell foam provides insulation and comfort off the ground. A DWR coating ensures moisture from the wet ground or bleachers doesn’t seep up and ruin your day. It’s compact and lightweight enough to roll up and carry in a daypack or toss in the car for a tailgate.

We guarantee the recipient already owns a camp mug—or two or three. They probably wouldn’t spend $110 on another one themselves. But that’s exactly why you should. Snow Peak’s matching golden set, inspired by autumn leaves, is equally at home around the campfire or on the kitchen counter, making everyday tea and coffee feel a little more special.

Everyone has a camera in their pocket, but how often do those videos make it out of the camera roll? The Camp Snap camcorder gives everyday moments the nostalgic treatment of an old-school home movie. Without a playback screen, the person behind the lens has to trust their instincts, just like back in the day. But a USB-C connection makes transferring footage easy. Perhaps this is the encouragement your loved one needs to finally make that ski film.

Surely your loved one already has a pair of tried-and-true trail shoes. But do they have these trail shoes? The collaboration between Québec footwear brand Norda and climbing gear maker Black Diamond was one of the year’s most exciting partnerships, and this limited-edition shoe is its crown jewel. Here’s your chance to give someone something they might have admired from afar but wouldn’t necessarily splurge on themselves.

All their gear has to go somewhere, and hopefully in an organized manner. The RUX Waterproof Duffel Box keeps everything contained in one durable, grab-and-go bag for impromptu trips to wherever. Its zipperless roll-top design expands or compresses to accommodate whatever they’re packing, while interior soft organizers keep smaller essentials from getting lost in the shuffle. The 840-denier TPU-coated nylon on the outside is absolutely bombproof.

Sorry, Grandma. We’ve got the knitwear covered this year. Dale of Norway re-created its 1994 Olympic ski sweater with the same Norwegian craftsmanship, but a thinner, softer 100 percent wool yarn in a unisex fit. This cozy layer is also a little piece of ski history for the powder chaser and forecast checker in your life.

Collaborations make especially good gifts, particularly when they’re limited to just 500 pieces. Japanese retailer BEAMS teamed up with REI to reimagine a parka inspired by the co-op’s original 1986 design, unearthed in its archives. For the person who loves a good vintage find, this is about as good as it gets.

A Look Inside the 2026 Dia Art Foundation Fall Gala

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Attendees included Sofia Coppola, Marion Cotillard, and Matthieu Blazy

Last night marked a busy evening in the world of fashion. In New York City, the 2026 CFDA Fashion Awards were hosted at the American Museum of Natural History, while in Los Angeles, a party was held to celebrate a new book documenting photographer Richard Avedon’s work in Harper’s Bazaar. But that wasn’t all—even more members of the fashion and art worlds united for the Dia Art Foundation’s annual fall gala in NYC.

The event acted as a celebration of two creatives—artist Roni Horn and British film director Steve McQueen—as well as a fundraiser for the arts. Guests gathered at the Dia Chelsea museum before migrating to a cocktail party and dinner where the honorees were toasted.

Some of the starry names in attendance included director Sofia Coppola and Oscar-winning actor Marion Cotillard. This is the first year that the gala has been sponsored by Chanel, after the French fashion house announced its partnership with the non-profit organization back in May. So, this naturally meant that Chanel’s current creative director, Matthieu Blazy, was also present (and he has been teaming up with the organization for years, even since he was at the helm of Bottega Veneta). In 2027, Chanel will sponsor the Dia Beacon gallery’s annual Spring Benefit luncheon, too.

Remarks were shared by the chairman of the Dia Foundation’s board of trustees, Nathalie de Gunzburg; the Dia director, Jessica Morgan; as well as by Matthew Barney, who paid tribute to Horn. Proceeds from the evening will go towards the foundation’s restoration projects, while also contributing to art-related lectures, commissions, educational programs, and more.

Matthew Perry Doc Premieres Atop Netflix’s Weekly English TV Rankings; ‘Lupin’ Part 4 Leads All Series As Non-English Fare Commands Attention

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Friends fans showed up on Netflix last week to watch the streamer’s new three-part documentary series on Matthew Perry.

The One About Matthew Perry was the top English-language television series on Netflix from October 26 to November 1, gathering 9.5 million views in its first weekend of availability. The series, which is the first in-depth doc about the late actor to feature new testimonials from loved ones and collaborators, was just over 30% ahead of Nobody Wants This in second place.

Season 3 of the Adam Brody and Kristen Bell-starrer was up a tad from its opening weekend performance with 7.2 million views. The audience was expected to go up, since this is the first full week of availability for Season 3. However, it’s still down from where Season 2 started.

On the English TV side of things, viewership slipped fairly significantly across the board from there. Below sank its teeth into third place with 3.8 million views, halving its audience from the week prior when it remained in No. 1. This marks the series’ fourth week in the Top 10 after attracting a record debut for a Canadian scripted series on Netflix. Watch on Deadline

Season 4 of The Diplomat also lost some viewership this week but remained in the Top 10 at No. 4 with 2.9 million views. Love Is Blind appears to be middling as well with 2.8 million views and the No. 5 spot even with new episodes premiering.

Lupin topped all series for the week as Part 4 climbed to the top of the non-English TV list with 10.8 million views. The return of the popular French drama also boosted all three previous installments back into the weekly Top 10 — Part 1 at No. 2 with 5.2 million views, Part 2 at No. 6 with 3.2 million views, and Part 3 at No. 8 with 3 million views.

The Omar Sy-starrer is still tracking below last season, though, when Part 3 raked in 14.1 million views in its second week of availability — yet another illustration of the ongoing struggles Netflix is facing with maintaining enthusiasm for scripted, returning series across the board.

Animals topped the English film list with a measly 4.7 million views. Much like on the series side, the non-English slate commanded much more attention. Mexican drama Surviving Hurricane Otis topped the non-English film list with 12.4 million views.

Skydance Inherited Massive Flops From Warner Bros. Deal - And Things Could Get Worse

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

There's a new mega-studio in Hollywood, and it's got a lot to prove. The Warner Bros. Discovery and Paramount merger was recently made official, with the new company named Skydance. This $111 billion deal has been met with intense scrutiny, in no small part because Skydance is now saddled with billions of dollars in debt. So, to make all this look even remotely tenable for the foreseeable future, that means Warner Bros. has to deliver at the box office.

Unfortunately, the new owners at Skydance inherited a string of massive flops ... and it may get worse before it gets better.

Indeed, a report from Page Six has examined Warner Bros.' ex-film bosses Michael De Luca and Pamela Abdy's "free spending" ways. Recall that Warner Bros. set a huge box office record in 2025, with six movies in a row earning at least $40 million on opening weekend. But this year has not been kind to the studio, with this report stating that Warner Bros.' 2026 slate will lose around $800 million. Can it really be that bad? Yes, it can.

Here's a full breakdown of Warner Bros.' 2026 at the box office, movie by movie:

"Wuthering Heights" — $80 million budget/$241 million worldwide

"The Bride!" — $90 million budget/$24 million worldwide

"They Will Kill You" — $10 million budget/$19 million worldwide

"Lee Cronin's The Mummy" — $22 million budget/$90 million worldwide

"Mortal Kombat II" — $80 million budget/$129 million worldwide

"Supergirl" — $170 million budget/$126 million worldwide

"Evil Dead Burn" — $20 million budget/$72 million worldwide

"The End of Oak Street" — $90 million budget/$121 million worldwide

"Practical Magic 2" — $75 million budget/$106 million worldwide

"Digger" — $180 million budget/$49 million worldwide

"Clayface" — $44 million budget/$84 million worldwide (still in theaters)

I previously broke down how movie box office actually works. Basically, without getting too far into the weeds, theaters keep around half of all money from ticket sales, and budget figures don't include marketing expenses. Taking that into account, we can see that only "Wuthering Heights," "Lee Cronin's The Mummy," "Evil Dead Rise," and "Clayface" will go down as hits for Warner Bros. this year, when all's said and done. And some of those are rather modest successes at best.

The rest of the slate is either filled with big disappointments or crushing flops. Tom Cruise's "Digger" will lose a lot of money at the box office, potentially as much as $250 million. That's just the tip of the iceberg, too. "The Bride!" also lost a fortune, while "Supergirl" flopped in a rather terrible fashion. Movies that were expected to be blockbuster hits instead became shipwrecks, with the debris washing up on the rocks. It's been bad.

The validity of that $800 million figure is somewhat irrelevant. It's crystal clear that WB's 2026 slate, overall, is going to lose a lot of money. Even if movies like "They Will Kill You" eventually made money on VOD/streaming, Skydance needs big hits. What it's gotten is a string of disastrous flops.

Even "One Battle After Another" was still a box office bomb ($213 million worldwide/$170 million budget) despite winning Best Picture at the Oscars. This Page Six report estimates it lost around $75 million. The Oscar helps, but probably not quite that much. Hence, Skydance needs Warner Bros. to deliver at the box office more than ever, and the hope is that 2027 will deliver green pastures. But that's far from a sure thing at this juncture.

On paper, there are some surefire bets on Warner Bros.' upcoming slate in 2027. 2024's "Godzilla x Kong: The New Empire" became the highest-grossing "Godzilla" movie ever, so it stands to reason that its sequel, "Godzilla x Kong: Supernova," will be a hit as well. But Warner Bros. will have to split that revenue with Legendary, so that's a somewhat qualified winner.

There's also the studio's much-anticipated return to Middle Earth. Even though Peter Jackson isn't directing "The Lord of the Rings: The Hunt for Gollum," it has a lot of appeal and revives a multi-billion-dollar global franchise. Optimistically, that should pan out pretty well, too, though how well remains to be seen. On top of that, there's the "Minecraft Movie" sequel, James Gunn's "Superman" follow-up "Man of Tomorrow," and "The Conjuring: First Communion," the latter of which ought to deliver. Remember, only six horror movie franchises have made over $1 billion at the box office, and The Conjuring Universe is one of them.

Outside of that? It gets far more murky. Director J.J. Abrams' "The Great Beyond" was pushed back to 2027, presumably because WB wanted an IMAX release. It's expensive and far from a sure thing. Even riskier is the new Nancy Meyers rom-com tentatively titled "Paris Paramount," which reportedly cost a dizzying $120 million. As for Sam Esmail's $60 million "Panic Carefully" and the CGI/live-action hybrid movie "Animal Friends?" They're big question marks.

For seemingly every sure thing on WB's 2027 slate, then, there's a real risk. Skydance film bosses Josh Greenstein and Dana Goldberg have a lot of decisions to make. For now, though, It's be less about the movies they want to make and more about how they're going to handle all the dicey prospects they've inherited.

A Fresh Look at Exes Will Smith and Sheree Zampino's Co-Parenting Bond

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

While Will Smith and Sheree Zampino got hitched and then divorced in the 90s, they've remained on good terms. See how they've continued their pursuit of happiness as co-parents.

Now this is a story all about how Will Smith and Sheree Zampino’s life got flipped, turned upside down.

And we'd like to take a minute, just sit right there, to tell you how The Fresh Prince of Bel-Air alum and the Hollywood Exes star went from married, to divorced, to co-parents who’ve remained on good terms. So good, in fact, that Smith and Zampino were recently spotted sharing a few laughs at the airport in Sydney, Australia, where he’d been filming his new movie Supermax.

Not that this is super shocking. After all, Smith’s wife Jada Pinkett Smith has noted that he and Zampino still go on trips together—not in a romantic way but in the sense that they’re still family as parents to 33-year-old son Trey.

"She and Will have their own relationship," the Girls Trip actress said on Sway’s Universe in 2018. "She’s gone on vacations with Will without me with Trey. Because I feel as though Trey needs to feel that dynamic between his two parents. That’s very important that he can feel that connection and where they do meet."

As for how they first met, it was back in the early 90s when Smith attended a taping of Different World. While the Men in Black star’s mission had originally been to impress Pinkett Smith, he ended up meeting Zampino.

"I didn’t meet Jada that night," he recalled in his 2021 memoir Will. "Sheree and I left together before the show was even over. We laughed all the way through dinner, all the way through that fall, and three months later, we were married."

Getting hitched in 1992, Smith and Zampino welcomed Trey later that year. And as their family grew, so did the actor’s career—with him landing the role in 1993’s Six Degrees of Separation shortly after they wed.

But ultimately, Smith and Zampino realized their pursuit of happiness wasn’t with each other. And on Valentine’s Day 1995, she filed for divorce.

Noting they tied the knot in their early 20s, "Marriage is not easy, especially at that age," Zampino said in a 2016 social media video. “Now, we’re trying to be parents, navigate through our marriage, I couldn’t reconcile all of that. Will was becoming bigger and bigger with his career. I’m becoming smaller and smaller and smaller. I didn’t have the tools. We didn’t have the tools to make it work. I left because I was unhappy. It’s really that simple.”

And Smith has been honest about the challenges he faced following their divorce.

"With Sheree and with Trey, that was a really difficult time," the Oscar winner noted on Pinkett Smith's Red Table Talk in 2020. "Divorce was the worst thing in my adult life. Divorce was the ultimate failure for me. I’ve been hurt a lot in my adult life, but I don’t think anything touches the failure of getting divorced from my 2-year-old son’s mother."

Smith went on to marry Pinkett Smith in 1997. They welcomed son Jaden the next year followed by daughter Willow in 2000. And along with Zampino and Trey, they learned how to become a blended family.

"We really had to figure it out along the way," Pinkett Smith shared on Red Table Talk in 2018. "For me, it really was just about maturity. Just not understanding the marital dynamic like, 'OK, well, divorce papers are sent, and people are over it, then this is done.' Guess what? It aint never done."

Because as she put it, "This woman is a part of this family. It’s not only taking on Trey, but Sheree was coming along too. It’s part of the package."

"There were times where we have bumped heads and it hasn't been pretty," Zampino admitted on Behind the Velvet Rope in 2022. “But listen, we get back on that horse and we press on. We do what we have to do. Because again, at the end of the day, it's about the kids. We have a responsibility to them."

And they have a desire to support each other too. Like how Zampino was the first guest on Pinkett Smith’s Red Table Talk and stood in Smith’s corner after he slapped Chris Rock at the 2020 Oscars.

"I hope people allow him to be human," she told DailyMail.com in 2022. "I really hope for that because I stand in support of him, we are on good terms."

Over the years, their family has gone through changes, including Zampino’s marriage and divorce from Terrell Fletcher as well as Smith and Pinkett’s separation.

"We are together," Pinkett Smith clarified on It’s Been a Minute in 2024, "but we are together in the way that works for us."

And it seems that Zampino and Smith will always be tied together too.  "I think that in terms of co-parenting, we’re more successful, a lot more successful actually, than in a marriage dynamic," she told Fox News in 2020. "We were put on this Earth to be co-parents. That’s what we did and that’s what we’re doing. We love each other and it’s been amazing. It’s been a blessing."

So no, Zampino didn’t agree with Smith calling their divorce a failure.

"I had an issue with that because I didn’t understand how we could do what we do as a blended family and do it as well as we do and as loving as we do and have an amazing son, and it’s looked at as a failure," she said on Red Table Talkin 2022. "It did what it was supposed to do, so that was successful."

And they're not the only ones chillin' out, maxin', relaxin' all cool. Keep reading to see more successful celebrity co-parents.

Though the Grammy winner and Swedish-Syrian artist called it quits in 2023, the pair continue to partner when it comes to their kids Lucia and Renn.

“We’ve actually had the most wonderful relationship,” Jwan told Attitude in September 2025. “It’s a very solid and calm co-parenting situation. We live 10 minutes away from each other. We speak every other day. We love our kids so much.”

He added, “It’s been an extremely lovely kind of experience. It’s not an experience—it’s a way of living.”

Megan Fox and Brian Austin Green have a body of co-parenting experience. So the Jennifer’s Body star and the Beverly Hills, 90210 alum—who share kids Noah, Bodhi and Journey—try to follow a key rule. "The number one," Brian told E! News in April 2024, "is always make sure that everything is centered around the experience of the kids." Because as he put it, a breakup is “gonna affect the kids. The only choice you have is how it affects the kids.” Part of that, Brian continued, is recognizing that it’s important to “pick your battles.”"The person that you were with that you're now separated from, you guys are separated for a reason," he added, "because you didn't get along. So, you can't expect to then get along after the fact in raising your kids. So, you have to decide, ‘OK, it's not about us getting along anymore. It's about us co-parenting, which is a totally different situation.'"Megan—whose divorce from Brian was finalized in 2022 two years after the filing—agrees. "I think it's really important when people separate to never ever disparage the other parent or even in a passive aggressive way make remarks," the Transformers actress said on a 2023 episode of The Drew Barrymore Show. "I don't let anything in my energy like that when I'm around my kids. Because if I don't accept and love their father, I'm rejecting a part of them because he's a part of who they are always. He's in their blood, and he's in their psyche, and they exist because of him."

Where is the love? Josh Duhamel and Fergie certainly have it for each other as co-parents."Fergie is an amazing woman. She really is," the Safe Haven actor said on a 2023 episode of The Jess Cagle Show, going on to share how the singer welcomed his wife Audra Mari into the family. "She's taken Audra in. And it could be much worse. That's the thing—you never know how these things are going to go."In fact, Josh said Fergie was thrilled for the couple after they announced in 2023 that they were expecting their first child together: Shepherd. The little one—born in January 2024—joined big brother Axl, whom the Las Vegas alum shares with the former Black Eyed Peas member."Because she's such a kind-hearted person, she really truly is, she only has love for Audra and is excited as—well, I can't believe how excited she is for us," continued Josh—whose divorce from Fergie was finalized in 2019, two years after they announced their split. "Like I said, it could be a lot worse. I've seen it, I've lived it. Thankfully, we have a really civil situation."Along with Axl and Shepherd, Josh shares daughter Rocca de Leon with Audra.

Irina Shayk, who shares daughter Lea with ex Bradley Cooper, isn't a big fan of the terminology.“I never understood the term co-parenting,” the model told ELLE in 2021. “When I’m with my daughter, I’m 100 percent a mother, and when she’s with her dad, he’s 100 percent her dad. Co-parenting is parenting.” Though Irina and Bradley split in 2019 after four years together, they've continued to stay on good terms. And even with their busy schedules, the runway vet and the Silver Linings Playbook actor have continued to go on family vacations, hang out in New York and support one another since their breakup.“He’s the best father Lea and I could dream of," she told the magazine in 2023. "It always works, but it always works because we make it work.”

As Mariah Carey put it, love takes time—and so can finding a rhythm in co-parenting. However, Nick Cannon, who shares twins Moroccan and Monroe with the Grammy winner, suggested they’ve settled into a good routine.“Mariah and I co-parent very well,” The Masked Singer host said on a 2022 episode of The Language of Love with Dr. Laura Berman. “We’re really great friends. She’s taught me so much. That’s family.” And Mariah seems proud of how far she and Nick have come since their split in 2014 and subsequent divorce."They spend time with him, and they have a good time; they spend time with me, and they have a good time," she explained to Harper's Bazaar in July 2025. "I want to make sure I’m always fair about the situation because it’s tough to grow up with divorced parents."In addition to being father to Moroccan and Monroe, Nick shares Zion Mixolydian, Zillion Heir and Beautiful Zeppelin with Abby De La Rosa; Golden “Sagon,” Powerful Queen and Rise Messiah with Brittany Bell, Legendary Love with Bre Tiesi, Onyx Ice with LaNisha Cole and Halo Marie and the late Zen with Alyssa Scott.

Of course, we have to include Gwyneth Paltrow and Chris Martin. After all, the Oscar winner and the Coldplay singer—who share kids Apple Martin and Moses Martin—did popularize the phrase “consciously uncouple” when they announced their split in 2014.“Chris and I committed to putting them first, and that’s harder than it looks,” Gwyneth said on a 2020 episode of The Drew Barrymore Show. “Because some days, you really don't want to be with the person that you’re getting divorced from. But if you're committed to having family dinner, then you do it. And you take a deep breath, and you look the person in the eye, and you remember your pact, and you smile, and you hug, and you make a joke, and you just recommit to this new relationship that you're trying to foster.”And the Goop mogul noted she and the musician will always be family."That's how it will be forever," she continued. "Some days it's not as good as it looks. We also have good days and bad days. But I think it's driving towards the same purpose of unity and love and really what's best for them. We have this idea that just because we break up we can't love the things about the person anymore that we loved and that's not true."

After divorcing in 2022, Gisele Bündchen and Tom Brady had to tackle a new challenge. “I think there’s easier days than others,” the supermodel, who shares kids Benjamin and Vivian with the former quarterback as well as a baby with husband Joaquim Valente, admitted in a 2024 interview with ABC News’ Robin Roberts, later adding, “They’re super smart children. They know what they can get away with. So I think it’s natural that it has different rules, and then kids just adapt. And they’re going to try to do what they want. And I can only control what I do.” And as Gisele put it, it’s all about balance.“Tom has time with them, and I have time with them,” she added, “which I think is amazing because they get to really experience—again, more-enriching for their lives—two different worlds, and they get to learn from two different worlds and that’s wonderful for them I think.” Tom also shed some light on how the exes are on the same team."I'm trying to raise them—we all are, myself and their mom—in a very loving way toward one another," the athlete, who also shares son Jack with ex Bridget Moynahan, told E! News in 2023, "to be very kind, to be empathetic and to have great perspective. Our kids are growing up in different ways than we grew up but also we want to raise them with the right values."

Jake Gyllenhaal's 124-Minute Sci-Fi Disaster Movie Is Officially Netflix's Biggest Streaming Hit

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

In 2004, "The Day After Tomorrow" clumsily delivered a message about climate change via a movie in which Dennis Quaid and Jake Gyllenhaal try to save the world from a new ice age. The questionable part of the film was the science, which as NASA Goddard Space Flight Center meteorologist J. Marshall Shepherd told USA Today, warranted "a D minus or an F." Otherwise, "The Day After Tomorrow" was a grand old time at the movies, and now it seems modern audiences are having a similarly wonderful time with the end of the world over on Netflix.

The sci-fi disaster film was co-written, co-produced, and directed by Roland Emmerich, who prior to this had taken a break from big-budget sci-fi fare to make "The Patriot" with Mel Gibson. "The Day After Tomorrow" saw him return to familiar territory, with a film based on on Art Bell and Whitley Strieber's 1999 book "The Coming Global Superstorm."

Dennis Quaid stars as Paleoclimatologist Jack Hall, who's dismayed to find that global warming has finally brought about a new ice age that's preceded by extreme weather events which batter major cities around the world. His son Sam Hall (Gyllenhaal) happens to be in New York just as the big freeze sets in, prompting Jack to set off on a rescue mission.

The movie isn't the most critically acclaimed thing Emmerich has ever done. But it did gross $552.6 million on a $125 million budget and remains an impressive showcase of early 2000s CGI and arguably the best natural disaster movie of the era. If it's been a while, or you're yet to catch "The Day After Tomorrow," why not join in the celebration over on Netflix?

"The Day After Tomorrow" hit Netflix on November 1, 2026 and immediately took over the streamer's charts like an unstoppable climactic event. Streaming viewership tracker FlixPatrol shows the movie debuted at number one on the Netflix Top 10 movie charts on November 2, 2026 in the United States. What's more it maintains that position at the time of writing and it will be interesting to see just how long Jake Gyllenhaal's disaster flick can hang on to the top spot.

Otherwise, there are plenty of other disaster movies streaming on Netflix, just in case you don't necessarily fancy the early 2000s stylings of Roland Emmerich and co.

Lego Reveals First Official Look at Huge $450 Godzilla Set

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The King of the Monsters is roaring to life in brick form

Godzilla is getting his first official Lego set, The Lego Group announced Tuesday. The new 5,363-piece set may not be the biggest Lego build ever, despite the King of the Monsters' stature, but it does set a new record for the toymaker: It's the biggest set yet to come out of the Lego Ideas program, which lets fans submit designs for community and official Lego approval.

Granted, the new Lego Ideas Godzilla set is tall. When posed with Godzilla's signature heat ray, the build stands an impressive 27.5 inches tall. Clear some shelf space.

The new Lego Ideas set is based on the Godzilla design from Legendary's MonsterVerse. "Textured scaly skin, jagged dorsal fins and a sweeping tail illustrate the creature’s imposing form, while his adjustable shoulders, elbows, wrists and fingers allow fans to manoeuvre the Monster into poses fit for any battle," The Lego Group said in a news release. New York-based designer Matthew "MattE720" Esposito created the original Lego Ideas build, which was approved by the Lego Ideas Review Board and is now becoming an official set.

“Building Lego Godzilla was a real passion project," Esposito said in a statement. "I’ve loved both Lego building and Godzilla for years, so I wanted to capture his shape, scale and character as accurately as I could. The head was definitely the biggest challenge, but seeing the model come together and eventually reach 10,000 supporters after more than two years was incredible. I’m so grateful to everyone who supported the project and helped make this dream a reality.”

Legendary's version of Godzilla has appeared in a quartet of films and the Apple TV series Monarch: Legacy of Monsters. Godzilla is poised to return in MonsterVerse form in 2027 with the release of Godzilla x Kong: Supernova. Toho's Godzilla is set to return to movie theaters this week with the release of Godzilla Minus Zero and the video game remaster of Godzilla: Destroy All Monsters Melee.

Lego enthusiasts who purchase the Lego Ideas Godzilla set between Nov. 27 and Nov. 30 will receive a free gift with purchase: the Lego Ideas Godzilla Logo and Footprint (21375) set.

Caleb Williams chooses to "calm things down" in shoe dispute with NFL

📅 2026-11-03  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The battle lines were drawn, in the form of an $11,941 fine for Week 6 and a $23,882 fine for Week 7. For Week 8, Bears quarterback Caleb Williams decided not to wear Caddix cleats that displayed the company’s logo — and exposed him to another, even larger fine.

It’s unclear whether he covered the Caddix name and logo, or whether he wore Caddix cleats that were made without any markings. Regardless, he chose to abandon the fight with Big Shield.

“Just to calm things down, man,” Williams told reporters after the 34-17 win over the Seahawks. “This was a big week, and [I’m] not here to, um, create any BS towards a team. You know, I want to keep that to a minimum for my side. Obviously, I get a lot of media.  And so, just try to minimize that as much as possible.”

He had gone from daring the league to fine him, twice, to daring the league to keep him from playing in his footwear of choice. All along, the answer was hiding in plain sight — hide the name and logo of the company that made the cleats.

Williams still doesn’t have a shoe deal, with Caddix or anyone else. Beyond reimbursing him for the fines (if the appeals fail), Caddix should be writing a sizable check to Caleb Williams. In one fell swoop, he gave the company far more exposure and positive publicity than the NFL’s official shoe partners have gotten for the millions they’ve paid to have their logos on the piece of equipment that few if any viewers ever even notice.

Sony Seemingly Winding Down PSVR2 Sales, Gone from USA Store

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Sony may be taking steps to wind down sales of its PSVR2 headset, as it's been spotted that the VR device is no longer being sold through some of its official regional outlets and at third-party retailers.

For a few weeks now, fans have been picking up on the fact PSVR2 no longer appears on the official PlayStation Direct website in the USA. I did a search of the website today, and the headset is nowhere to be seen.

If you scroll down the PS5 accessories tab on the USA webpage, you'll find a PSVR2 heading, but the actual device isn't there. You only receive options to purchase the charging station, the PC adapter, and a third-party case:

Besides these examples within the PSVR2 tab, I can find absolutely no evidence the VR device exists on the PlayStation Direct site for USA customers. For now, at least, the PSVR2 headset is still for sale on the UK side of PlayStation Direct.

The situation goes further, though, according to Road to VR, which has done a deep dive into regional data relating to PSVR2. According to them, the removal of the headset from regional PlayStation Direct pages began in mid-October, which is when fans started noticing it was gone. The link that should have taken you to the PSVR2 purchase page was producing a 404 error.

As the outlet also highlights, retailers like Amazon, Best Buy, and Walmart do have stock listed, but it comes from third-party retailers at considerable markups over the normal price of the headset — around $750 at Best Buy.

It appears more difficult than ever to buy a PSVR2 at its recommended retail price.

News Sony Seemingly Ends VR Ambitions by Selling Patents to Meta

To get a better gauge on what's happening, it'll be interesting to see if any PSVR2 discounts are revealed around Black Friday. Last year, Sony knocked $100 off the price for the spending period. It'll be interesting to see if anything happens this month — and if there's even stock to facilitate a discount.

Of course, PlayStation's first-party support for PSVR2 has been woeful. Third-parties have brought their games to the VR platform, but Sony called it a day on launch, after Horizon Call of the Mountain, in terms of dedicated PSVR2 titles. Most recently, it sold its VR patents to Meta, which hammered another nail into any VR efforts from Sony in the future.

Liam grew up with a PlayStation controller in his hands and a love for Metal Gear Solid. Nowadays, he's found playing the latest and greatest PS5 games as well as supporting Derby County. That last detail is his downfall.

Hardly surprising. Not sure how many components it has in common with PS5, but I’d imagine that Sony wouldn’t want to take away from manufacturing PS5s. I don’t have a PSVR 2, but I think it’s a damn shame that Sony just left it out to die.

It has also been removed from the Japanese Direct store as well as the hardware section of the US PlayStation app. To completely remove the web pages and all links to them is too much work if this is just a temporary stock issue. That shows a deliberate decision of discontinuing the device.
