Before He Played Sylvester Stallone in ‘I Play Rocky,’ Star Anthony Ippolito Fought Hard for His Own Big Break

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

I don’t think I was supposed to hear Anthony Ippolito when he exclaimed, “20 minutes?” right before this interview, as he was given the amount of time this interview would last. Though, it kind of hit me, if you’re not used to doing interviews — and there’s no way Ippolito can be at this young stage of his career — 20 minutes is a long time to make conversation with a total stranger. Especially, as he is quick to point out, after a barrage of quick, three-minute interviews.

Ippolito has been in films and television before — kind of famously now, he played Al Pacino in “The Offer” — but none of those were roles in which he’d have to do press. Let alone playing the lead role in a major motion picture in which everyone wants to talk to him. Oh, and that lead role is as … Sylvester Stallone. Related Stories ‘Okinawa Saudade’ Review: A Moving Documentary About the Okinawan-Brazilian Community Explores the History of Displacement AT&T and IndieWire’s Filmmaker Party at AFI Fest Celebrated Filmmakers and Those Who Champion Them

In “I Play Rocky,” yes, Ippolito plays Stallone at the make or break point in Stallone’s career. Stallone had written “Rocky,” a very in-demand script. Not in demand was the idea of Stallone playing Rocky — as MGM offers more and more money to Stallone in an effort to get him to not play Rocky. (If you don’t know, Stallone stuck to his guns, played Rocky, and is now a very wealthy and famous actor.)

Directed by Peter Farrelly, Ippolito says he sent in multiple tapes trying to land this role — which does seem to be in the same spirit as the film itself. Though, Ippolito said, when he was actually going through the casting process, that’s not at all what he was thinking, even though it may seem that way now.

Ahead, he tells us (yes, over the course of 20 minutes) the Stallone movies he used to prep for playing Stallone, what is was like to talk to Stallone before filming, and why he loves Stallone and Dolly Parton together in “Rhinestone.” Also, if he had to play Stallone again in another movie, he does have another “I Play” installment in mind…

The following interview has been edited and condensed for clarity and length.

IndieWire: You seem excited about this being 20 minutes?

It’s a little strange, I’ve been having three-minute increment conversations with somebody. So, yes, I am excited.

OK, so how many Stallone movies did you watch? Did you stick to that era? His voice kind of evolves by the late ‘80s.

Yeah, I really tried to stay focused on a period of time. I don’t know the exact number. More so, from the stuff I was looking at I was watching it over and over again, rewatching. With the exception of a few.

Well, “Rocky Balboa.” It’s one of the best movies of the franchise.

For people who haven’t seen that one in a while, it’s very sweet.

It really is. It really is. And he does some excellent work in it. And I think it’s really powerful that he was able to come back many years later, after the fifth one, and make this incredible movie.

From closer to the “Rocky” era, I kind of love him in “The Prisoner of Second Avenue.” Jack Lemmon thinks he stole his wallet…

It’s just scene after scene of Jack Lemmon chasing Stallone through Central Park trying to get his wallet back…

But then Jack Lemmon realizes he left his wallet at home and wound up being the one who mugged Stallone.

But he was mistaken for a mugger and this was part of his issue, right?

It’s funny watching it now, because it’s so obviously Sylvester Stallone. So it’s amusing the whole role is just him being chased.

I agree with you and I found that throughout a lot of the stuff I was unearthing throughout this process.

In that, if he hadn’t become famous, I’d still look at him and think, “what an interesting looking dude,” and wonder what happened to him.

Speaking of that, there were times during “I Play Rocky,” knowing full well what happens in real life, and I’m still like, “Maybe just take the money. That’s a lot.”

It’s really remarkable. Especially when you convert the amount from what it was then to what it would be now.

I know you made a video for your audition which involved an old punching bag and gloves. What were you saying in it?

Oh, well, there was certain dialogue I was coming up with, or thinking of coming up with. And I hadn’t had my hands on too much of the script at this point. So I was just trying to capture his physicality and his movement as a boxer. I was just really passionate about the project. I wanted to put myself out there in a way. And, thankfully, I was able to get my hands on the script a bit later on.

So are you doing lines from “Rocky”? Or just stuff you came up with on your own?

I ask because that’s a time when you haven’t gained the weight yet, so you’re not in the physical condition you are when filming. So I feel you had to really sell this dialogue? Which obviously worked.

Yeah, I mean … it was difficult to come up with that part of it. I had given myself inspiration with some parts of the original film, and some parts from what I felt would work for dialogue from what I was understanding of the story. And then once I was able to get the script, then I was able to just put scenes on tape. And then continuously send them stuff until they relented.

Oh, there was more than one? You just kept going?

Well, that’s the spirit of this movie. Did you feel that way when you were sending them in? “Well, that’s what Stallone did.”

Hm … maybe? You know, it seems that way kind of now?

Right. But at that time, I was just like, “I probably seem crazy to everyone who is on the other end of this.”

Yeah, but I wasn’t thinking about that part for some reason, I guess.

Which is probably good, if you were getting ahead of yourself it doesn’t work out the way it did?

I know you spoke to Stallone before filming. A year ago, he was saying he knew nothing about this movie. When and how did that change?

Peter Farrelly tells a funny story about that. He had actually met with Stallone prior to that and discussed his level of involvement. And they discussed how Stallone would kind of not be necessarily in the trenches with us, just for the sake of Peter not wanting people to feel like Stallone had his hand in it in a way that it was shaped…

And historically Stallone never does that. I say that sarcastically because I’m well aware he has a specific vision for any movie he’s involved in…

Right. Right. So, Stallone went back after that and told somebody he has no idea about it. And Peter was like, “What are you doing?” And Stallone said, “You told me to separate myself.” And Peter was like, “Not that far, man!” So, I guess that was just a bit of a communication breakdown.

So in Stallone’s mind, he was doing a favor by saying that?

I’m not sure, but I was able to speak with him about a month before we started shooting. And I’m very grateful he was willing to take the time

What was that like? Was he in good spirits or was he apprehensive?

He was in great spirits about it. I was nervous, as you could probably imagine.

Yeah, and not only that, I had done so much of my own research. So, up to that point, it was really built up as this really big thing in my head. So, I was just really, really excited to meet him and to listen to him. He was really, really generous and disarming and very willing to share what that period of time was like for him.

What did he tell you that you don’t know already?

Oh, man. Well, he discussed with me building the character of Rocky as a writer. That’s where it became really interesting to me. Because he had studied filmmaking and writing while being an usher. So, hearing his perspective and his kind of clinical breakdown of how he approaches a script and of a character? It was hugely informative in developing and my understanding of this side of him as a writer.

From that, what changed on how you approached playing him? How does that conversation inform what we see?

You just really understand that this thing is his baby. And it really makes more sense that these numbers going so high during these negotiations because the script is that good. And so well written. Yeah, he’s a terrific writer and I think he proved that to Chartoff and Winkler with “Paradise Alley,” first.

My only experience with him was at the afterparty for “The Family Stallone.” He shows up at this restaurant in Little Italy and it’s like the Red Sea parting. Everyone stopped talking and made room so he could walk by. When he got near me I said, “Hey, Sly,” which I immediately regretted. He stopped, looked at me, and gave me a little nod.

He then walked off and I thought, “You know, good enough.”

Are you glad that you said it now? I feel like I would be.

But you keep bringing up meeting Wim Wenders. I know you’re a fan of his, is there something maybe happening?

I got to meet him at the Zurich Film Festival.

I know you like his movies, are you going to work with him?

I just think he makes great films, he’s made great films and he was a really, really sweet man and we got to speak. That’s pretty much it.

I feel that way about so many. Well, Scorsese obviously. Richard Linklater, Derek Cianfrance, Tim Burton.

That’s an eclectic group. “Place Beyond the Pines” and “Beetlejuice” are very different movies.

Very different movies. Well, I like that, You can go in so many different directions. And I appreciate directors and filmmakers who are singular.

And that is kind of the thing about Stallone, too. Did you look at the movies Stallone directed?

Honestly, well, I know he directed all the “Rocky” movies other than the first and fifth ones. And I understand he directed more stuff past that as well. But I hadn’t specifically looked at stuff he directed for this.

How far back did you go back to watch with Peter Farrelly’s films? Did you go back to “Dumb & Dumber”?

I’ve seen so much of his stuff already. So there’s not one that jumped out at me, but also, obviously, “There’s Something About Mary.”

It really is. I think he really killed it with that one. I mean, he has such an incredible sensibility. And he’s always aware of something on set. If there’s a moment to be discovered, to make a meal out of it, he’ll find it.

I always think about, “Unless someone comes up with six-minute abs.”

On social media a lot of people are doing their own “I Play …,” then insert any other Stallone movie type posts. If you had to play him again, what movie would you want to do?

Like, “I Play Cobra,” would be interesting. I do love “Cobra.”

You know, I guess I’m not the right age for this, but “I Play Rocky Balboa” would be very interesting…

Well, it’s the first one I remember. I would have had to have seen the ones prior? To the point I’m in the theater for “Rocky Balboa.”

Well, not necessarily. The first one I saw was “Rocky III”…

Because it was the one on cable all the time. Then I was told, “Oh, you know, the first one is a lot different.”

Yeah, I was pretty young, but I do remember seeing “Rocky Balboa.” And I just think there’s something interesting about the fact that, similar to how he’s fighting the boxing commission at the time to let him fight, because they think that he can’t, or shouldn’t — I think he was having a similar dialogue with the studio at the time. About him making the movie in the first place.

It’s interesting, even at that point, they still didn’t think they could take a chance on him making a “Rocky” movie.

I guess, yeah. It’s strange, but history does repeat itself.

Ah, OK, it’s not streaming, so it’s a little harder to find. It’s just interesting this is only a few years after “Rocky” and now he’s on stage singing with Dolly Parton. It’s kind of fascination what that one decision did for him…

It’s very true. And I think Dolly Parton is spectacular in that.

How good is she in that? Right? I mean, she has such a warmth to her. I mean, yeah. She’s just incredible in the movie and it makes it so fun. The two of them! They are dynamic.

You could also make “I Play Nick Martinelli.”

So what are the lessons you personally took away from playing Stallone? I can’t help but think there are lessons here for actors starting out.

I agree. Well, he really overcame so much and he didn’t have too many people in his corner at the time. I mean, thankfully, he had Sasha who was there for him, who AnnaSophia Robb plays in the movie. And she’s so great in it. And as much as you want to see Stallone persevere, and be kind of a lone wolf through this? For actors there’s meaning in that, and there’s a lesson to be learned in that.

But there’s also a lesson to be found in Sasha. Because you need that person sometimes in your life. Or sometimes it’s good to be that person for people — to remind people to believe in themselves. Because, no matter what, Stallone overcame so much, but of course he doubted himself. And if you don’t have at least somebody who is there, willing to be that person to remind you to believe in yourself, it’s a tough road.

Amazon MGM Studios will release “I Play Rocky” in select theaters on Friday, November 6, before going wide on Friday, November 20.

The Best LED Light Strips of 2026, Tested and Reviewed

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Old Houses AREN’T Scary: What We’ve Learned & Loved About Them

By Stacey L. Nash, Additional research provided by Timothy Dale

Published Nov 3, 2026 9:30 AM EST

We may earn revenue from the products available on this page and participate in affiliate programs. Learn More ›

LED light strips are long circuit boards with surface-mount device light-emitting diodes (SMD LEDs) that can be stuck almost anywhere you want extra illumination. They’re commonly used as recessed lights, television backlighting, or under-cabinet lighting, and many pair with smart-home connections for convenient, customizable operation.

To see which LED lighting strips are worth their prices and installation times, we put 10 popular options through a series of hands-on tests and sought advice from multiple experts. After testing each of the following lights, we rated them based on their ease of use, the attractiveness of their colors and modes, and the added value of their special features.

Based on our test results, we granted the Govee RGB 32.8-Foot LED Strip Lights the award for Best Overall. They are simple to install and operate, bright enough for task lighting, and very reasonably priced. Keep reading to see why we like each of the following options and to get additional information on how to choose the best LED light strips for your walls and ceilings or car and closet.

Bottom line: Explore the best LED light strips of 2026, tested for ease of use, color options, and special features, to find the perfect fit for your space.

No matter where you’re aiming to add an LED light strip, you want to be sure the strip you select is high quality. Based on our tests for installation, ease of use, and effectiveness, the following products are some of the best LED light strips you can buy.

The Govee 32.8-foot smart LED lights offer an incredible 16 million color options through the Govee home app. It also connects with Amazon Alexa and Google Home, enabling voice control for hands-free operation. You can turn the lights on or off as you enter a room or adjust the color settings without getting up.

These smart LED strip lights come as two flexible 16.4-foot rolls that require 36 watts of power for optimal functionality. With the app, you can set daily schedules or activate the sound-responsive microphone, which prompts the LEDs to change color alongside music, hand claps, and other sounds.

We were impressed by how well these programmable LED lights connected to the app for choosing scheduling and lighting options, including manual preferences or preset colors based on seasons, moods, holidays, or special occasions. What really put this Govee LED light strip over the top was the inclusion of adhesive or screw-on support clips that help support heavier sections of the electrical cord for a neat and secure setup.

What our tester says: “This Govee strip balances all the key features you need in an LED light strip: adhesive strength, color options, and scheduling. We love having them under our kitchen cabinets since they stay put without falling and are bright enough for food prep.”—Stacey L. Nash, Product Reviews tester and writer

Get the Govee 32.8-foot LED light strips at Amazon.

Bring pops of brilliant light and color to almost any size space with the Tenmiro RGB 100-foot color-changing LED strip lights. With two rolls of 50-foot strip lights, users can install enough lighting for a medium to large room and choose from millions of customizable colorways using the 44-key remote or the brand’s app.

In testing, we enjoyed the music-sync function of these 60-watt LED strip lights. They boast a built-in sensitivity-adjustable microphone that causes color to dance to the rhythm of any song (as long as it’s on the iTunes app). You can set a timer on these lights for automatic on/off, and the dimmable feature sets the light level based on the chosen settings. There are also scene options, including preset colors based on seasons, moods, holidays, or occasions, and modes that use various strobe effects.

The adhesive backing of these lights allows them to stick to any clean, dry surface. The adhesive is strong, and in testing, we appreciated that the lights held firmly, even with the weight of the Bluetooth transmitter.

Get the Tenmiro LED light strips at Amazon.

This Pangton Villa light kit is specifically designed for backlighting TVs and computer monitors, and our tests proved just how important that design is for such a purpose. The product comes as four adhesive-backed strips attached to one another via flexible connectors that adjust to fit any TV or monitor between 40 and 60 inches (there’s a larger size for 65- to 75-inch screens).  Since light strips aren’t generally flexible enough to make effective 90-degree angles, the connectors make a big difference in creating precise, clean corners. Once we fit and attached the strip set to our TV, it didn’t budge at all during our tests.

In addition to making sure you choose the right size kit for your screen, you’ll also want to check your TV or monitor’s ports to ensure compatibility. This kit connects directly via USB, but if your screen doesn’t have a USB port, you’ll need to buy a separate adapter for a power outlet. Once connected, you can choose from 16 colors or dim the lights using the included remote control. Though the lights were always responsive to the remote during our tests, it took us a minute to learn what each button did, so be patient as you scroll through each option.

Get the Pangton Villa LED light strips at Amazon.

Motion-activated automatic lights make entering dark spaces safer and less scary (at any age). Though these bar-style lights aren’t the same flexible, color-changing LED strip lights as the rest of the options on our list, we found them to be a worthy addition for their ease of use and reliable performance.

The VyanLight motion-sensor LED lights attach to virtually any surface via an adhesive-backed magnetic mounting strip. Simply install four AAA batteries, stick the strip to the wall, and slide the light into the strip. When the time comes to replace the batteries, it’s easy to detach the light rather than having to take the whole strip down. In the meantime, you can trust them to turn on any time you’re within 9 feet of them and off after about 20 seconds. That is, unless it’s already light in the area, as their daylight sensors keep them off during the day to protect battery life.

Though ideal for illuminating dark areas for safety, the strips’ size—7.5 inches long by 1.2 inches wide—also makes them a good option inside wardrobes and closets as well as under cabinets, which is where we tested them. Since these are battery-powered strip LED lights that are completely wireless, you can install them virtually anywhere. Plus, the strips are made with high-quality aluminum for durability; they seem unlikely to crack, bend, or erode over time.

What our tester says: “We’ve had battery-operated wireless LED strip lights like this before, and taking them down to put in new batteries isn’t always easy. Being able to simply detach these lights from their magnetic mounting strips is so much better.”—Stacey L. Nash, Product Reviews tester and writer

Get the VyanLight LED light strips at Amazon.

Extra illumination in a car’s interior can increase safety, visibility, and ambience, and these dual-line light strips from Govee are designed to deliver on all those fronts. They have rubber tubing that protects against water, moisture, and abrasions, so you can run them under seats, along the dashboard, or against the flooring. They’re easy to install by simply plugging them into your vehicle’s parallel double outlet and have short-circuit protection for longevity.

While screws and zip ties are included, most cars don’t have good places to attach the cords to keep them out of the way. During testing, we had cords trailing through our car that were easy to catch and tangle. So long as we steered clear of them, however, we loved using the Govee app’s DIY mode to create custom light settings to match our car’s accessories and inhabitant’s moods. When driving in dark areas, we appreciated being able to dim the lights using Bluetooth connectivity.

We also liked the light’s dynamic music mode, which uses a built-in microphone to sync the lights to the stereo to create vibrant dancing colors around the interior. The lights also have a smart color-picking feature that customizes them to an uploaded photo to create a totally custom color scheme.

Get the Govee smart car LED light strips at Amazon.

Want to make any room feel like a dance club? Consider installing the Daybetter Bluetooth LED strip lights on the ceiling. This set of two 50-foot strip lights has Bluetooth connectivity through Apollo Lighting, Daybetter’s app, for adjusting the six preset and five standard colorways (variable colorways are also adjustable with a dial). The lights also include a remote control for adjusting color and brightness.

We recommend this set of Bluetooth smart light strips in rooms where you like to play music, as the music-sync mode manipulates the lighting color and movement to the beat of the music. Plus, the smart timing setting lets you preset times to turn the lights on or off automatically. The disco downside? The lights sync only with music on iTunes.

In testing, these LED lights proved easy to install thanks to the adhesive on the back of the strips and the plug-in design. However, be sure to follow the manufacturer’s instructions for how to install LED strip lights because dust can interfere with adherence. Also, be aware that you may need an extension cord since the electrical cord isn’t super long, which was the case for us and detracted from the ambience. We occasionally had issues with app connectivity when the Bluetooth connection was up near the ceiling.

Get the Daybetter Bluetooth LED light strips at Amazon or Walmart.

Those looking to add a little ambience and extra illumination to the kitchen and other splash-prone spaces might want to check out the Daybetter waterproof LED strip lights. Though calling them truly “waterproof” is inaccurate, these LED lights are covered in resin, which makes them durable and somewhat splash-resistant.

Although these lights are advertised as being suitable indoor or outdoor LED strip lights, the instructions specify that they are not intended for humid environments or open air. Not all elements are waterproof, so we cannot recommend them for use outside or even in the bathroom (and we were naturally a bit put off by the conflicting claims). However, we did place them above our kitchen sink, and when the light strips got splashed by a mix of water and grease, they did not fail.

Be aware that the protective coating on these strips makes them stiff. We had a hard time getting them to follow curves, and they pulled away from 90-degree corners we tried to attach them to during testing. If you’re looking for a water-resistant option for linear installation, however, these are great.

What our tester says: “The illumination and color options on these lights are excellent. They don’t connect to the Daybetter app, but we like the simple yet comprehensive 44-key remote. There are also only 16 color options instead of millions, yet we never struggle to find one to match the mood.”—Stacey L. Nash, Product Reviews tester and writer

Get the Daybetter waterproof LED light strips at Amazon.

Unfortunately, the following two LED light strips we tested didn’t make our list:

To test the best LED light strips, we installed them in appropriate places, including our kitchen, office, TV, and car. We also spoke with Dara Greaney, CEO and founder of LED Light Expert, who provided some sage advice for shoppers. “Strip lights are a fun DIY project, but they are not plug and play. You need to figure out how to secure them, where to power them, and how to cut them if necessary.”

Then, over the following 2½ weeks, we used the light strips via remote and app to test their features and functionality. We set lighting schedules, created lighting themes, and used the lights in real-life situations. We rated each set according to installation, illumination, and durability criteria. After rating the lights on a scale of 0 to 4, we calculated their scores and assigned the worthy options awards.

There are many features to consider when shopping for the best LED strip lights, from color and size to intended use and brightness. Before shoppers purchase an LED light strip, they will want to keep the following factors in mind to ensure their choice meets or exceeds their expectations for the intended application.

It’s essential to determine where the LED light strip will be installed before deciding which product to purchase. Some designs are for specific purposes, such as stair lighting or television backlighting. This specialization differentiates those products from standard LED light strips.

For example, a product intended for backlighting a TV may come with four precut pieces and television-specific syncing options that change color to mimic the colors on the screen. Other location considerations include water resistance for patios, dimmer compatibility for bedrooms, or motion sensing for a hall, bathroom, or stairway.

Light strips are available in a variety of lengths, typically from 1 to 32 feet, though some strips run up to 50 feet long or more. The brightness and intended purpose usually correlate to their length. For example, LED light strips intended for television backlighting or use on stairs tend to be shorter for easier installation.

Low-brightness LED light strips can be up to 32 feet long with about 300 LED lights. High-brightness LED light strips may have the same number of LED modules, but they’ll usually only be about 16 feet long. However, most flexible LED strip lights can be cut using scissors to accommodate small areas. If longer LED tape is needed, check to see if you can connect pieces to create the desired length.

Most light strips feature a flexible circuit board that can bend around corners or run over curved surfaces. Some LED light strips are so flexible that they can be bent up to 90 degrees without damaging the circuit board.

However, as the power of an LED light strip increases, the flexibility decreases, with some light strips being completely rigid in design. These inflexible products tend to be much shorter, sometimes only 1 foot long, and significantly brighter than the average light strip. They can also feature motion-sensing technology, making them ideal for lighting stairs.

As with LED light bulbs, the brightness of an LED light strip is measured in lumens and is dictated by the density of the LED lights within 1 meter.

Ideal brightness can be challenging to figure out, and some manufacturers don’t include this information. By taking note of the LED density, you can determine the average brightness of the light strip. Most light strips come in two densities: standard density (SD) of 30 LEDs per meter (9.1 LEDs/foot) or high density (HD) of 60 LEDs per meter (18.3 LEDs/foot).

SD light strips produce an average of 540 lumens and need 27 watts of power. They can be up to 32.8 feet long. HD light strips provide an average of 1,080 lumens, need 40 watts of power, and come in a maximum length of 16.4 feet.

Some light strips come in only one color, while others can have color selections that range in the millions. The color option chosen is entirely dependent on personal preference.

Color temperature in various types of light bulbs is measured in degrees Kelvin (K) and describes how warm or cool a light’s color appears; lower numbers indicate warmer color temperatures. The soft glow of an average incandescent light bulb is a warm temperature of 2,700K, while natural daylight is much cooler, averaging 6,500K.

We interviewed interior decorator Vivianne Chow, the owner of Viv and Tim Home in Chicago, on this subject. She shared some expert advice. “Make sure that the color temperature of the LED light strips matches what you currently have at home. I recommend sticking to 2700K or 3000K for a warm white look instead of 5000K, which is a little bit too cool-toned.”

Wattage measures how much energy the light strip requires to function at its optimal brightness. If a light strip requires more energy than the power source can provide, it won’t work correctly, causing the light produced to be weak. LED light strips generally operate using batteries or power adapters plugged into wall outlets, but some options can be hardwired directly into the home’s electrical system.

LED light strips with lower brightness requirements, such as a 540-lumen strip, will also have reduced wattage requirements. As brightness demands increase, power demands also rise, with a 16.4-foot SD light strip requiring 27 watts of power and a 16.4-foot HD LED light strip requiring 40 watts.

In our interview with Dara Greaney, he also mentioned that “LEDs are 90 percent more efficient than incandescent and 60 percent more than CFL/fluorescent, so anytime we use LEDs we are saving money and going green.” LED lights are certainly something to consider for energy-conscious consumers.

In years past, LED light strips were hardwired into the electrical system to work with a light switch or plugged into a power outlet and controlled with a button. However, LED light strips have come a long way from their original design.

Light strips are available that connect to Bluetooth, Wi-Fi, or remotes. With these controls, you can adjust brightness and color, program the lights to sync with music, or set schedules for waking up, arriving home from work, or going to sleep.

LED light strips that connect with smart-home devices like Amazon Alexa or Google Home can even be controlled using voice commands, while more straightforward options with just one color may only have a remote to turn the light on or off.

LED light strips come with many pros and a few cons. Whether you’re looking for LED strip lights for bedrooms, LED strip lights for kitchen cabinets, or bedroom LED light strips, the answers to these common questions may help.

LED light strips are safe as long as they’re installed and operated according to the manufacturer’s instructions. For example, indoor lights shouldn’t be installed outside, and some lights should not be installed where they can get wet.

LED light strips can run for hours on end without being shut off. They get warm, but they’re designed to underdrive the LEDs, meaning they don’t put out much heat. This makes them nice ambient or task lighting but not ideal as LED grow lights.

Yes, you must plug in most LED light strips. We did test one battery-operated cordless model, but it was a short 16-inch strip. However, this non-electric option would be a reliable light during a power outage.

Almost every LED strip is compatible with a dimmer switch. To make an LED light strip dimmable, you must first connect its input wire to a dimmer unit and then connect the unit to the power supply.

To create ambient light in your home with LED strip lights, the optimal locations are under or above cabinets and counters in the kitchen, behind mirrors in the bathroom, or even around large pieces of furniture like bookshelves and home entertainment units.

LED strip lights can be disposed of by taking them to a local recycling center.

LED lights are built to last, with most LED light strips lasting for up to 50,000 hours of use, which is roughly 10 to 20 years of runtime.

Nathan Fielder’s Elizabeth Holmes Documentary, <i><em>You Can See Everything</em></i>: What We Know So Far

📅 Sep 09, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Elizabeth Holmes’s story is getting told in a new project with Nathan Fielder at the helm. The comedian has released his new documentary titled You Can See Everything about Holmes, founder of the now-defunct biotech company Theranos, before she began serving her 11-year prison sentence for committing fraud.

Unlike a typical documentary, the film features Fielder chatting with Holmes in a Q&A format, while an unnamed A-list actress reenacts the events of the documentary in asides as Holmes. Here’s everything we know about You Can See Everything.

The official logline for the documentary reads: “34 days before she’s sent to prison, notorious Silicon Valley founder Elizabeth Holmes invites a skeptical film crew to document her every move. What begins as an intimate portrait of an enigmatic felon becomes a mind-bending three-year journey into the abyss. From boundary-pushing directors Nathan Fielder and Lance Oppenheim comes You Can See Everything.”

Audiences were buzzing at Telluride Film Festival, where the doc premiered with a surprise screening on September 6. Variety called the doc “a shameless, fascinating, reality-TV-eats-its-tail look at entitled duplicity” and said “the sensation of ‘WTF? I’ve got to watch this!’ did not subside as the movie went on. It only intensified.”

Deadline reported that “the nearly three-hour-long film drew continuous laughter, frequently triggered by hilarious reaction shots of Fielder...attempting to process what Holmes and Evans [Holmes’s partner] are pushing.” And the L.A. Times said the movie contains “mind-bending twists that meld reality and performance.”

The documentary is currently available to watch in theaters, but it will likely hit HBO Max when it comes to streaming. Warner Bros. Discovery owns HBO and HBO Max, and the company has an agreement that states A24 titles will automatically go to HBO Max as their first stop on streaming after the theatrical window and PVOD (Premium Video on Demand) release windows are over. You Can See Everything currently has no streaming release date.

In addition to Fielder and Holmes, The Hollywood Reporter confirmed that an unnamed “A-list actress” has a role in the making of the documentary. Her identity has not been revealed, as “the filmmakers and fest [Telluride Film Festival] requested that her identity be kept secret until more people have a chance to see the movie.”

As for what viewers can expect from the A-list actress, THR reports her cameo is “the craziest twist of a crazy film,” but her involvement is accidental. After agreeing to meet with Fielder, she finds herself wanting to be involved in the filming process and “winds up becoming a full-fledged collaborator in the film, appearing throughout the rest of it alternatingly as herself, giving commentary on the insanity of the situation, and then in reenactments—long Fielder’s go-to way of making sense of things—as Holmes.”

The first official trailer for the documentary arrived on September 8, featuring Fielder and Holmes having an unsettling conversation.

“I don’t have anything to deceive you on. Of course I’m not deceiving you. I’m engaging with you as a human being. Why would I deceive you? There’s no...there’s no reason for me to do that,” Holmes tells Fielder.

“You’re being real right now?” he asks her, to which Holmes replies, “I’m always being real.”

A first-look image was also released with the trailer, which shows Holmes preparing to give an item to a child, while Fielder looks at her from the background.

The first official trailer arrived on September 23 and provides a close look into some of the conversations that Fielder and Holmes had with each other throughout the film.

“You really feel you did nothing wrong?” Fielder asks her, to which Holmes replies, “I did not.”

He presses her and follows up the question with, “So where did things get lost? How are you where you are right now?”

Holmes finds herself at a loss: “I wish I knew. I don’t know. I don’t feel like I understand that,” she tells him.

At another point in the trailer, Fielder questions how Holmes could be so calm about his filming her every move. When he asks Holmes if she has any “worry” about him being at her home 24/7, she answers, “I don’t think so. I think this is the moment in time to capture this story before I’m gone.”

The comedian also manages to interview Holmes’s partner, William “Billy” Evans, who reveals he fell in love with her in their first conversation. “Weren’t you worried she was scamming you?” Fielder asks.

Evans coolly responds, “100 percent, but what does she has to gain from me?”

When Fielder reminds him that his family is wealthy—Evans is the heir to the Evans Hotel Group—he responds, “I think there’s a lot of different levels of wealth.”

Neither Holmes nor Evans have seen the doc, Oppenheim revealed during an October Q&A after the film’s Los Angeles screening. “Neither of them have seen the film. Elizabeth is still incarcerated; Billy hasn’t seen it,” the director said, with The Hollywood Reporter adding that Evans has “a strained relationship with the crew.”

Fielder added that Evans “started trying to exert a level of control over the process that made it very hard to engage with him, and it was uncomfortable for us and also A24 and everyone. And I think we sort of had to stop—I think communication broke down basically, and we’ve been in that like state for a little bit. So it’s been challenging in that way.”

On November 2, Evans revealed the “one misconception” about his relationship with Holmes following the release of the movie. “That I somehow control Liz. Anyone who actually knows her would find that idea hilarious,” he responded to an X user.

Evans added, “She’s fiercely independent, strong willed, and my best friend. We are partners in every sense. We challenge each other, disagree, and face the world together. Her closest friends are also some of my closest friends. If I ever mistreated her, they’d have my head.”

Yes. In 2019, HBO released The Inventor: Out for Blood in Silicon Valley, which explored the rise and fall of Holmes and her multibillion-dollar company, Theranos.

In 2022, The Dropout—also about the rise and fall of Holmes and Theranos—premiered on Hulu. Amanda Seyfried starred as Holmes in the miniseries.

20 Fantastic Animated Series That Outshine Most Live-Action Shows

📅 Feb 19, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

It's widely agreed by most that animated film and television are incredibly and unfairly disregarded by many as true art. So much more can be accomplished visually and stylistically in the realm of animation. It's especially impressive when there's a great animated television show, requiring not just a longer runtime to animate, but typically released every week.

Animated shows are so wonderful. Being serialized projects doesn't make them only for kids, though, like typical Saturday morning cartoons are (however, some of these are). If animated television has shown the world anything, it's that animation can be for everyone, kids and adults — sometimes even adults only. While some may see them as just "cartoons" and nothing to take too seriously, in fact, many animated products often find themselves being better than what one would find in live-action projects.

With the X-Men coming to the Marvel Cinematic Universe, people are all theorizing and wondering what the plot could be like for this new iteration. With the cast that's been announced being so young, many people have been hoping — praying — that they take from the likes of the animated series, X-Men: Evolution.

This series brings the iconic characters into a much younger age, making them high schoolers, rather than teachers at Xavier's School for Gifted Youngsters. Making the main team so much younger than they are typically seen brings the opportunity for all-new kinds of interpersonal drama that can't be found outside of it. It makes one wonder why they aren't depicted as teenagers more often (it also kills the controversial love triangle that is commonly between Wolverine, Jean Grey, and Cyclops—thank goodness).

While 2011 may seem like a disastrous year for the Green Lantern character, many don't realize that was only in cinema. The hero had an animated series that began the same year that is genuinely one of the best adaptations of the Hal Jordan (Josh Keaton) character. Overshadowed by the terrible Green Lantern movie, though, Green Lantern: The Animated Series was never given the shot it deserved.

The way this series adapts the entire Green Lantern Corps is almost perfect. It manages to have some mature themes and stories while still being wholly accessible for younger audiences. It feels like a space opera in its scale, but still juggles personal relationships and character arcs as if it were a grounded street-level show somehow.

If someone hasn't already heard the comic book fans of the world raving about 1992's X-Men: The Animated Series, they've probably been living under a rock for a few decades. It's so popular that it even got revived with Disney+'s X-Men '97—people love it that much. For good reason, too, as X-Men: The Animated Series has some of the best superhero storytelling on television.

This five-year-long show has some of the deepest, most meaningful storytelling in animated superhero television. The X-Men have always had this over most Marvel Comics properties. The show accurately and truthfully depicts themes and messages about discrimination and bigotry while still being an action-packed, kid-friendly romp.

One of the most popular modern anime is none other than the 2016 series My Hero Academia. Kohei Horikoshi—the mangaka of the franchise—does an exceptional job blending typical Western superhero tropes and character archetypes with the shonen anime style. My Hero Academia is different than what most people expect from a superhero show, with a unique premise, world, and characters that can only be found in this franchise.

The premise of a young boy in a mostly superpowered society who has no abilities getting the greatest power on the planet from the literal Symbol of Peace is already engrossing. But when mixed with the one-of-a-kind characters surrounding him — with equally special power sets — and emotionally compelling stories and character arcs, My Hero Academia becomes a truly distinctive and special series.

From the exceptional minds of Greg Weisman and Brandon Vietti, Young Justice is one of those shows that truly captures the teenage experience (much like another popular show of Weisman's). Taking a page out of Teen Titans, Young Justice depicts a team of sidekicks and young heroes coming together to form a team of their own.

Unlike Teen Titans, this show goes even deeper with its mature storytelling — less anime-inspired. When it comes to good animated DC Comics content, Young Justice is up there with the best of the best. The storytelling at play, as well as the character arcs, make Young Justice one of the best DC Comics animated properties out there.

It was always going to be next to impossible to perfectly follow up Avatar: The Last Airbender, but with this in mind, the studios behind both that series and The Legend of Korra did pretty dang well when it came to developing the latter. The sequel series, following an all-new Avatar, Korra (Janet Varney), is not perfect, but still an exceptional show, at the end of the day.

People give The Legend of Korra a lot of flak for simply not being the same series Avatar: The Last Airbender was. Expecting the follow-up of one of the best television series of all time to be even better is the definition of overly wishful thinking. At the end of the day, though, The Legend of Korra still finds itself being better than a plethora of series out there, thanks to how well-written it is, especially when it comes to the development of the show's world and characters.

Kids who grew up on Regular Show consistently call back to it even today, thanks to how hilarious it is. Many attribute Regular Show to their appreciation for absurdist humor, and absolutely adore the wonderful Mordecai (J. G. Quintel) and Rigby (William Salyers), along with the numerous characters that surround them.

Regular Show gets used as a meme—people use the absurd situations that Mordecai and Rigby consistently find themselves in on a typical Tuesday as a joke, all the time, which has kept the memory of the show alive. People simply love this show, and some even beg for it to make a comeback somehow, despite the ending of the original series being incredibly good and satisfying as it is.

One of the most beloved superhero animated series of the last few years is, without a doubt, Invincible. Based on the Skybound comic book of the same name, written by Robert Kirkman, this show came to Prime Video and captured the hearts of the world almost immediately. Despite some failings in the animation department, at times, Invincible still remains one of the most popular shows on the air at the moment.

Thanks to the work of some amazing vocal performances from the likes of Steven Yeun, J.K. Simmons, Gillian Jacobs, Seth Rogen, Walton Goggins, and many more, the characters pop off the screen and come to life. This, combined with some great storytelling thanks to Kirkman and the new writers on the writing team, makes Invincible such a great watch.

One of the greatest accomplishments to come from Disney Channel/Disney XD is, without a doubt, the ever-iconic Gravity Falls. Brought to the screen by Alex Hirsch, this animated series does an abundance of things right. It crafts a compelling mystery across the entire plot, while crafting just as interesting ones each week in a serialized format.

There's genuinely so much to love about this show. Despite being an animated show on Disney Channel/Disney XD, anyone can watch and enjoy Gravity Falls. The humor is stellar, the voice acting is lively, the story is engaging, and all of the character arcs are compelling. Not to mention, it went the extra mile to get audiences invested by hiding little hints, secrets, clues, and more into the show that could be used to discover more — the intro theme has a backwards message at the end, which changed each season to act as another cipher.

One of the greatest anime ever made comes from the studio that made the extremely popular My Hero Academia, Studio Bones. After a first series that was pretty mixed, they brought the Fullmetal Alchemist franchise back in 2009 with a show that was more accurate and faithful to the original manga, which was called Fullmetal Alchemist: Brotherhood.

Thanks to how amazing the manga by Hiromu Arakawa is, Fullmetal Alchemist: Brotherhood has found itself being one of the most beloved anime of the modern age, with a whopping and clean 100% on Rotten Tomatoes. It's such an excellently told story that audiences can't help but get engaged and care about it. Not to mention, Studio Bones animated it phenomenally well.

Four powers, and four completely different kinds of person behind them. Here is what you would wake up with, what it would cost you, and why the MCU keeps handing it to somebody exactly like you.

You get the power that is really about taking the hit so that nobody else has to. Strength in the MCU always lands on people who were already carrying too much, which is exactly why it suits you.

You get the power that never switches off. Spider-sense is a gift and a nervous system on permanent alert, and the people who get it in these films are always the ones who were already worrying about everybody.

You get the quietest power in the building, and quietly the most useful. Invisibility comes with force fields for a reason, because the people who prefer not to be seen are usually the ones holding a shield around everybody else.

You get the biggest and least comfortable power on the list, the one that runs directly on feeling. Chaos magic is not really a superpower in the MCU so much as an emotional state with consequences attached.

All About Saoirse Ronan’s Husband, Jack Lowden

📅 Jul 28, 2024  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Saoirse Ronan and Jack Lowden have been together for over five years. They tend to keep their lives together very private, but have posted some shots on social media as their relationship progressed.

In July 2024, it was reported that the couple had gotten secretly married. With just a few friends and family members present, they had a ceremony in Edinburgh, Scotland, around the weekend of July 20, 2024. In spring 2025, Ronan revealed her first pregnancy, and the couple was seen pushing a stroller that September.

Here’s everything to know about Lowden and his relationship with Ronan so far.

Lowden is also an actor from Scotland, with a long career onstage. In 2013, he won both an Olivier Award and the Ian Charleson Award for his role as Oswald in Ibsen’s Ghosts. He’s also had some significant roles in film and TV, including Christopher Nolan’s Dunkirk.

In 2020, he bought a home in west Cork with Ronan, but they also live in London and have a terrier named Fran. The couple shares a production company with producer Dominic Norris. Their film The Outrun stars Ronan.

In December 2018, reports surfaced that they were dating after they were seen together at an after-party at Central Park’s Tavern on the Green. The pair played husband and wife in the film Mary Queen of Scots. Sightings of them over the next few years were sparse, and they infrequently appear together on social media. In 2020, they were photographed out in London. In 2022, they were spotted at the Australian Open together. On New Year’s Eve 2023, Lowden shared a mirror selfie of them to celebrate.

In 2019, a reporter for The Telegraph asked Ronan, “Am I allowed to ask if you’re going out with the person that everyone thinks you’re going out with?”

Earlier that year, Lowden was asked about Ronan by ES Magazine and answered the same way, but with more words.

“Oh I’m not going to talk about that. I don’t like talking about that side of life,” he said. “Most of the actors I know live pretty normal lives, it’s just every now and again they have to do something ridiculous or extraordinary. My life hasn’t changed. People seem more interested—I guess that’s changed, for sure.”

Lowden has gushed about Ronan when talking about her acting, including in an interview with i newspaper.

“The woman is a force of nature, on- and offscreen, and utterly fearless and a brilliant leader as well,” he said. “My first love is actors, and when you’re given a Ferrari like her, it’s all about how you make everything good for the Ferrari to show off. Don’t make a Ferrari do what a Renault Clio does. It’s a dreadful analogy, but they’re like gold dust, good actors. When there’s someone like that, you just have to facilitate them and you will get gold. And my God, did we.”

He praised her again in a March 2025 interview with The Times, saying that seeing her perform in Mary Queen of Scots inspired him to keep acting and not quit. “There was me at 27 thinking about stopping because I felt self-conscious or like I didn’t know how to do it,” he said. “And then watching this 23-year-old carry the weight of a huge film like that with ease and grace and majesty. It was remarkable to watch. She makes it look so easy, like breathing. It was totally inspiring.”

The pair made their debut on the carpet as a married couple at the 76th Primetime Emmy Awards at the Peacock Theater in Los Angeles on Sept. 15, 2024. Lowden wore a classic black tux with satin lapels. Ronan was wearing a custom Louis Vuitton two-piece with a sleeveless top and trailing skirt. Around her neck, she wore a stunning diamond necklace by Bulgari.

In an October 2024 interview with USA Today, Ronan shared that she once thought she’d “never” have a partner or a “beautiful network” of people because her career kept her so busy.

“My life has become so much more than just work,” Ronan said. “For a long time, that’s all I did; that’s how I met people and made sense of the world.”

She continued, “I feel like I’m part of a group, which I never thought I would have. I used to think, ‘You’ll never have friends. You’ll never have a partner.’ I was so in my own world, in a lot of ways. So now, to feel like I’ve got a very rich personal and social life—it’s so precious to me.”

In March 2025, Lowden offered rare comments on his relationship with Ronan in his The Times interview. When asked how they keep their marriage strong as two actors whose jobs sometimes require travel, he said, “[We] just have to check in with each other a lot. Obviously it made it a lot easier when we worked on the same project.”

They plan to work together again, he added, “but the question is, how do we do it, because Saoirse, she has ambitions as well to direct.”

On May 22, 2025, Ronan made an appearance at the Louis Vuitton Cruise at Palais des Papes in Avignon, France, wearing a semi-sheer dress by the fashion house that revealed she and Lowden are expecting their first child. The silky black gown was covered in matching black lace trim and folded around her body, showing off her black sandals under the asymmetrical skirt.

On May 31, the couple was seen in London. Ronan wore a form-fitting black bodysuit that appeared to show off her pregnant figure, under a puffer jacket and a sweater tied around her waist. She had on a baseball cap and sunglasses. Lowden was dressed in black sweatpants and an Adidas hoodie and was carrying a backpack.

On Sept. 11, 2025, Hello! reported that the couple had quietly welcomed their first baby. According to the outlet, Ronan and Lowden were seen earlier this month in London pushing a stroller. Lowden later spoke about becoming a father in his November 2026 GQ cover story.

Speaking to People, Ronan revealed that she hopes to act alongside her husband in the future. “I mean, I would love for us to work together again. I’ve said to him I really want him to do a play with me because he’s so incredible onstage, and that’s where he really comes to life,” she said.

“I think that’s where he feels at his best, and it’s not necessarily where I feel at my best, so I would really like to do it with him so he can hold my hand a little bit,” Ronan continued. “I mean, listen, we met through work and we’ve produced stuff together, and we’re very much a part of one another’s work life as well as personal life.”

Still, Ronan said Lowden needs a little more convincing. “It just feels like when the right thing comes along, it would be the obvious choice for us to do it together. But I think he’s more hesitant to work with me than I am with him,” she said. “I don’t know. I think he just finds it weird. He finds it weird working with any of his friends. He gets embarrassed.”

Lowden praised his wife’s acting and opened up about being a father for the first time in his GQ profile.

He reflected on watching her shoot a film in Italy that summer. “I’m going to sound a bit soppy, but getting to watch her act, in the flesh—that’s how we met—is really wonderful,” he said. “She’s so at home doing it.”

He also spoke about welcoming their first child in 2025. “Since I was a kid, I always knew I wanted to be a dad, and now I am,” he admitted. “You become aware that you’ll watch your kid grow up—sounds morbid—and you think, ‘Oh my God, I’m in charge of a kid, and I’m still a kid myself.’”
